For the primary efficacy assessment beta quantification LDL-C, i.e., LDL-C directly measured using ultracentrifugation, was used; if a beta quantification LDL-C was missing, calculated LDL-C could be used instead.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Participants were in a fasted state for all efficacy laboratory assessments. From randomisation (Day 1) until Day 360, the results were blinded to participants, investigator staff, persons performing the assessments, and the clinical trial team.

In addition, carotid intima-media thickness (cIMT) was measured locally at Day 1 (baseline), Day 330 (Year 1), and Day 720 (EoS) by ultrasonography. This assessment was exploratory / hypothesisgenerating in nature.

PK assessment was based on sparse PK sampling (i.e. 3 samples at the Day 1 visit; pre-dose immediately before the first administration of study drug, 4-, and 24-hours after first dose). Concentration-time data were summarised using descriptive statistics. (see sections 8.2.1 Methods and 8.2.2 Pharmacokinetics)

All participants were invited to optional pharmacogenetic analyses of the underlying causal mutation(s) of their HoFH. Samples were analysed for potential mutations in the LDLR, ApoB, PCSK9, and LDLRAP1 genes.

The key safety assessments consisted of monitoring and recording of all adverse events (AEs), laboratory evaluations (haematology, biochemistry, urinalysis, pregnancy tests), anti-drug antibody (ADA) measurement, vital signs, growth (height, weight, body mass index [BMI]), and pubertal development (sex hormones, Tanner staging). Other safety evaluations were physical examinations, neurological evaluations, and electrocardiograms (ECGs).

The primary endpoint was the percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to the end of the doubleblind treatment period at day 330 (year 1).

Secondary endpoints included time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330, absolute changes in LDL-C and other lipid parameters during Part 1, as well as assessments from baseline to the end of the open-label Part 2, i.e. day 720.

Exploratory endpoints included individual responsiveness of participants, changes in LDL-C by type of underlying causal mutation, change in carotid intima-media thickness (cIMT), reduced frequency of LDL-apheresis treatment at Day 720 (Year 2) compared to baseline, and evaluation of inclisiran pharmacokinetics (PK) based on sparse data.

The endpoints are considered to be in line with previous inclisiran studies in adults.

## Sample size

There was no formal sample size calculation. The sample size for this study was selected based on feasibility; the planned sample size was approximately 12 adolescent participants. In Part 1 of the study, 13 eligible participants (9 inclisiran, 4 placebo) were enrolled and analysed. All 13 randomised participants entered Study Part 2 and were analysed.

## Randomisation and blinding (masking)

Part 1 of the study was randomised, double-blind and placebo-controlled. Participants, investigator staff, persons performing the assessments, and the clinical trial team were blinded to the identity of the treatment from the time of randomisation until at least database lock for all Part 1 (year 1) data. Exceptions were the independent statistician and programmer to prepare interim analysis reports for the DMC, and the PK analyst to avoid unnecessary analysis of placebo samples; in addition, a PK interim analysis (IA) team had access to unblinded individual-level PK/baseline data in order to update a PK extrapolation to paediatrics before the Part 1 database lock to support early planning of a dose selection strategy for paediatric studies in younger children. Part 2 of the study had a single-arm, open-label design. After baseline visit (Day 1), participants were randomised by interactive response technology in a 2:1 ratio to receive double-blind s.c. injections of inclisiran sodium 300 mg or placebo in Part 1 (year 1) of the study (days 1, 90, 270). On day 360 (\"switch day\"), participants randomised to placebo in Part 1 received inclisiran and participants randomised to inclisiran in Part 1 received placebo. This dose remained blinded in order to maintain the blind for Part 1 of the study. Subsequently, all participants received open-label s.c. injections of inclisiran sodium 300 mg in Part 2 (year 2). Investigational and control drugs were supplied by the MAH as single-use pre-filled syringes for s.c. injection. Placebo was supplied as sterile normal saline (0.9% sodium chloride in water for injection) and administered as a 1.5 mL s.c. injection to match the dose in the inclisiran arm. The placebo prefilled syringes were blinded and were identical in appearance to their active counterparts.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Statistical methods

Efficacy and safety analyses of Part 1 data were performed after all participants completed the Day 360 visit or discontinued prior to the Day 360 visit; analyses of Part 2 data were performed after all participants completed the Day 720 (EoS) visit or discontinued prior to the Day 720 visit. In cases where the dosing time at Day 360 did not allow to determine whether data belonged to Part 1 or 2, the data were considered to belong to Part 1. The following data sets were analysed. The Screened Set (SCR) included all participants who provided study informed consent. The Randomised Set (RAN) consisted of all participants who received a randomisation number, regardless of whether double-blind treatment was received or not. The Full Analysis Set (FAS) consisted of all randomised participants with the exception of those mis-randomised participants who did not receive study drug; mis-randomised participants were defined as those who were not qualified for randomization but were inadvertently randomised into the study. According to the intent to treat principle, participants were analysed according to the treatment they were assigned to during the randomisation procedure. The FAS was used in analyses for the primary, secondary, and exploratory efficacy objectives in Part 1 of the study. The Safety Set (SAF) included all participants who received at least 1 dose of study treatment. Participants were analysed according to the study treatment received. This was the primary population for the safety analyses in Part 1. The SAF allowed the inclusion of nonrandomised participants who received double-blind treatment in error. The PK analysis Set included all randomised participants who had at least 1 PK measurement. Participants were analysed according to the treatment they received. This was the primary population for the PK analyses. The Open-label Phase Analysis Set (OAS) included all participants who entered Part 2 of the study and received at least 1 dose of study treatment in Part 2. The OAS was used to analyse data from participants who entered Part 2. For the primary endpoint percentage change in LDL-C from pre-dose baseline measurement to Day 330, summary statistics to characterise change from baseline in LDL-C by treatment group and the difference between treatment groups along with 95% confidence interval (CI) at the Day 330 time point were used. The CI was constructed assuming Students t-distribution and equal variance between the 2 samples. No formal hypothesis testing was planned. No remaining intercurrent events were expected. The primary endpoint did not have missing data imputed. Only data from the Day 330 visit time point and baseline data needed to evaluate change from baseline in LDL-C were included in the primary analysis. No sensitivity analyses were planned. As a supplementary analysis, the main

analysis, after excluding the participant receiving LDL-apheresis during the study, was repeated.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Summary statistics by treatment group and treatment difference along with 95% CI was provided. No subgroup analysis was performed.

For the secondary endpoints time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330 (year 1), defined as the average of percent changes from baseline to Days 150, 270, and 330, percent and absolute change in LDL-C, Apo B, Lp(a), non-HDL-C, total cholesterol, triglycerides, HDL-C, VLDL-C, Apo A1, and PCSK9 from baseline to each assessment time up to Day 720 (Year 2), and incidence, severity, and relationship to study drug of treatment-emergent AEs and SAEs; vital signs; laboratory parameters; ADA measurement; growth (height, weight, BMI); pubertal development (sex hormones and Tanner staging) descriptive summaries by treatment group were presented.

Additionally, the mean treatment difference and associated 95% CI was provided for time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330, for absolute change in LDL-C at Day 330, and for percentage change in Apo B, Lp(a), non-HDL-C, total cholesterol, and PCSK9 at Day 330. To construct the CI for the time-adjusted percent change analysis, the patient-level averages assuming Students t-distribution and equal variance were used, as for the primary analysis. No formal hypothesis testing was performed.

For exploratory variables, frequencies and percentages of participants in the categories LDL-C &lt;50 mg/dL, &lt;70 mg/dL, &lt;100 mg/dL, and &lt;130 mg/dL, as well as reductions of ≥10% and ≥20% LDL-C from baseline were summarised by treatment group at Day 330 (Year 1) and Day 720 (Year 2). In addition, as supportive analyses, &gt;15% LDL-C reduction from baseline was summarised by treatment group at Day 330 and Day 720, and ≥30% LDL-C reduction from baseline at Day 720. For participants receiving LDL-apheresis, the number and percentage of participants with a reduced frequency of LDLapheresis was presented by treatment group at Day 720. Summary statistics for LDL-C percent and absolute change from baseline were provided by treatment group in subgroups defined by underlying genetic mutation(s). Values of cIMT were summarised at each time point by treatment group. Change in cIMT from baseline to Day 330 (Year 1) and to Day 720 (Year 2) were summarised by treatment group.

As regards PK analyses, inclisiran plasma concentration data were listed by treatment, participant, and

sampling time point using descriptive statistics (see section Pharmacokinetic data analysis). further refine the extrapolation from adult PK data with adolescent PK data) as part of developing a

An interim analysis on PK data was performed to update a PK extrapolation to paediatrics (i.e., to dose selection strategy for planned future paediatric studies in younger children &lt;12 years of age.

For safety analyses, the number and percentage of participants who had treatment-emergent AEs, treatment-emergent SAEs, injection site reactions, and treatment-emergent events related to other safety topics (including cardiac / hepatic / hypersensitivity / musculoskeletal / neurological / renal / psychiatric / new onset or worsening of diabetes) were summarised. For laboratory data and vital signs, absolute values and change from baseline were summarised. The number and percentage of participants with categorical criteria were summarised. In addition, the number and percentage of participants with newly occurring notable abnormalities for selected laboratory parameters and vital signs were summarised. The number and percentage of participants with treatment-induced transient and persistent ADA and no treatment-induced ADA were summarised. Shift from baseline in Tanner stage was summarised by sex, treatment, and visit. Shift in menarche status from baseline was summarised by visit and treatment group.

Adequate statistical methods were applied. Due to the small sample size, only summary statistics were provided. No formal hypothesis testing was planned.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Results

## Participant flow, numbers analysed, and recruitment

Study ORION-13 was started on 16 February 2021 (first participant first visit) and completed 18 November 2024 (last participant last visit); the CSR version 1.0 is dated 05 March 2025.

A total of 20 participants were screened and 13 were enrolled and randomised, 9 in the inclisiran and 4 in the placebo group. All 13 participants completed Part 1, entered Part 2, and were analysed. The planned full study duration was 748 days.

All 13 participants randomised, 9 in the inclisiran and 4 in the placebo group, completed Part 1 and Part 2, and were analysed.

## Conduct of the study

The study protocol was amended twice. Key features of each amendment are given in the table below. There were no other changes to the study conduct.

Table 13: Protocol amendments

| Version and date         | Summary of key changes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|--------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Amendment 1, 01-Oct-2020 | The purpose of Amendment 1 was to modify participant eligibility criteria regarding HoFH. Based on the mechanism of action of inclisiran and in order to avoid exposing patients who are unlikely to respond to study medication, patients with a null (negative) mutation in both LDLR alleles were excluded from study participation and all participants were required to have a genetic diagnosis of HoFH. Additionally, participants with a history of poor response to therapy with any monoclonal antibody directed towards PCSK9 were excluded. Amendment 1 was issued before the original protocol was sent to the IRBs/IECs and Health Authorities, i.e., before any participants were screened or randomised. |
| Amendment 2, 17-Feb-2023 | The purpose of Amendment 2 was to add an IA for the PK data collected on Day 1, to update a PK extrapolation to paediatrics, i.e., to further refine the extrapolation from adult PK data with adolescent PK data, covering a lower range of body weights and ages. The updated PK extrapolation was critical as part of developing a dose selection strategy for planned future paediatric studies in younger children (<12 years). The PK IA was performed by a separate team (not involved in any other study activities) before the Part 1/Year 1 database lock, when the study was unblinded as per protocol. This amendment was issued after completion of recruitment.                                            |

There were no changes to the planned protocol specified analysis. In an earlier synoptic CSR, which provided results of the Part 1/Year 1 analysis of the study, screening values were used for weight and height baseline calculations, while in the present final CSR, for reasons of consistency, the baseline values (i.e., last measurement collected on or prior to the first dose of double-blind treatment) were used.

The changes in the planned conduct of the study are not considered to have influenced the outcome of study ORION-13.

## Baseline data

Demographic and baseline characteristics were overall comparable between the treatment groups. Approximately two thirds of the randomised participants were female (9/13; 69.2%) and the mean age was 14.8 years (range 12 to 17 years). Most participants were White (11/13; 84.6%) and the remaining 2/13 (15.4%) participants Asian, 1 in per group. The mean (±SD) BMI was 20.8 (±3.9) kg/m 2 . Mean (±SD) baseline LDL-C and PCSK9 levels of study participants were 272.2 (±111.2) mg/dL

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

and 501.4 (±102.3) ng/mL, respectively. All (100%) participants received statin treatment and 11/13 (84.6%) were treated additionally with ezetimibe with or without other lipid lowering therapies; 1 participant in the placebo group was on LDL-apheresis. All participants had HoFH, which was diagnosed genetically as required per protocol. There were no important differences in genotypes between treatment groups, with most participants having either a LDLR defective/defective or LDLR null/defective genotype, as expected.

No participants had a medical history of pre-diabetes, type 1 diabetes, type 2 diabetes, hypertension, coronary heart disease, cerebrovascular disease, peripheral artery disease, or chronic heart failure. However, 1 (1/13; 7.7%) participant in the inclisiran group met the fasting plasma glucose (FPG) laboratory criteria for pre-diabetes; no participants met the corresponding laboratory criteria for diabetes. All participants had never smoked or used e-liquids. One (1/13; 7.7%) participant in the placebo group had a current . No participants in either treatment group had partial or complete statin intolerance. Overall, 4/13 (30.8%) participants were treated with an anti-PCSK9 monoclonal antibody, evolocumab or alirocumab, as a prior medication, 2/9 (22.2%) in the inclisiran and 2/4 (50.0%) in the placebo group; treatment had ended &gt;90 days before the screening visit as required per protocol.

During the double-blind Part 1, 10/13 (76.9%) participants had been taking at least 1 concomitant medication, 7/9 (77.8%) in the inclisiran and 3/4 (75.0%) in the placebo group. The most common drug classes used were anilides, corticosteroids, COVID-19 vaccines, and oral bivalent iron preparations (all 30.8%). There was 1 change in lipid modifying therapy (LMT) from rosuvastatin 20 mg to atorvastatin 20 mg in the placebo group. During the open-label Part 2, 9/13 (69.2%) participants had been taking at least 1 concomitant medication, 6/9 (66.7%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 3/4 (75.0%) in the placebo-inclisiran group. The most common drug classes used were anilides and propionic acid derivatives (each 30.8%) and ascorbic acid plain and oral bivalent iron preparations (each 23.1%). The only new or changed LMT during Part 2 was a change from atorvastatin 40 mg to rosuvastatin 40 mg.

In Part 1, 2/13 (15.4%) participants reported at least 1 protocol deviation, both in the placebo group. The protocol deviations concerned 2 changes in apheresis schedule / duration and a change in the type of background statin medication (rosuvastatin 20 mg to atorvastatin 20 mg). There were no protocol deviations during Part 2.

As regards duration of study treatment exposure, in Part 1 as well as in Part 2, all (100%) participants were administered all 3 doses of study drug (inclisiran or placebo). Mean duration on study and mean duration of exposure to study treatment in Part 1 were both 360.6 days in the inclisiran and 364.0 days in the placebo group and both 358.5 days in Part 2, with similar durations in the inclisiraninclisiran and placebo-inclisiran groups. In Part 1 and Part 2 combined, the mean duration on study and the mean duration of exposure to study treatment were both 719.1 days, with similar durations in the inclisiran-inclisiran and placebo-inclisiran groups. The overall patient years of exposure to study treatment was 25.6, 17.7 in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 7.9 in the placebo-inclisiran group. Dose modifications and dose interruptions were not permitted and did not occur.

Demographic and baseline characteristics were comparable between treatment groups. Approximately two thirds of the randomised participants were female (9/13; 69.2%) and the mean age was 14.8 years (range 12 to 17 years). One (1) participant in the placebo group was on LDL-apheresis. All participants had HoFH diagnosed genetically and there were no important differences in genotypes between groups.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary analysis

The mean percent change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 was -21.6% in the inclisiran and +11.7% in the placebo group, with a mean between-group difference (95% CI) of -33.25% (-59.17, -7.34). While formal hypothesis testing was not planned, the 95% CI indicates nominal statistical significance. For further details see table below.

A supplementary analysis excluding participants (n=1) on LDL-apheresis treatment yielded consistent results, with a mean between-group difference (95% CI) of -37.72% (- 67.37, - 8.08).

Table 14: Primary Efficacy Endpoint: Percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330Part 1 Primary Analysis (Full Analysis Set)

|                                            | Inclisiran N=9         | Placebo N=4   |
|--------------------------------------------|------------------------|---------------|
| Percentage Change from Baseline to Day 330 |                        |               |
| n                                          | 9                      | 4             |
| Mean ± SD                                  | -21.6 ± 13.36          | 11.7 ± 30.52  |
| Median                                     | -20.9                  | 3.6           |
| Q1, Q3                                     | -30.9, -14.5           | -8.6, 32.0    |
| Min, Max                                   | -39.7, -1.9            | -15.4, 54.9   |
| Mean difference from Placebo (95% CI)      | -33.25 (-59.17, -7.34) |               |

Source: [Study ORION-13 - Table 14.2-1.1i]

A waterfall plot of individual percentage changes in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 by participant is provided in the figure below. While of variable magnitude, all participants in the inclisiran group showed a reduction in LDL-C, with the highest change being -39.7%. The response in the placebo group was less consistent; one participant showed a large increase in LDL-C (54.9%), while another participant showed a decrease (-15.4%). LDL-C reductions greater than 15% are considered a clinically relevant improvement with a PCSK9 directed therapy according to the 2023 EAS consensus statement update on HoFH (Cuchel et al., 2023). Two thirds (66.7%) of the participants in the inclisiran group achieved a &gt;15% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 and more than half (55.6%) had a ≥20% improvement in LDL-C.

For study ORION-13 no formal hypothesis testing was planned. The mean percent change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 was -21.6% in the inclisiran and +11.7% in the placebo group (mean between-group difference [95% CI] -33.25% [-59.17, -7.34]). A supplementary analysis excluding the participant (n=1) on LDL-apheresis showed consistent results.

A waterfall plot of individual percentage changes in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 by participant showed a variable magnitude; all participants in the inclisiran group showed a reduction in LDL-C albeit with significant differences in magnitude, while response in the placebo group was less consistent with an increase in LDL-C of 54.9%, as well as a decrease of -15.4%.

Two thirds (66.7%) of the participants in the inclisiran group achieved a &gt;15% and more than half (55.6%) a ≥20% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330.

## Secondary analyses

In line with the primary efficacy endpoint, the mean time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C (after Day 90 up to Day 330) was -21.0% in the inclisiran and 13.0% in the placebo group, with a betweengroup difference (95% CI) of -33.97% (-67.54, -0.39) (see table below). A supplementary analysis excluding the participant on apheresis showed consistent results with the primary analysis.

Table 15: Secondary efficacy endpoint - Time adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330- Part 1 Primary Analysis (Full Analysis Set)

| Time adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330 |                        |               |
| n                                                                                     | 9                      | 4             |
| Mean ± SD                                                                             | -21.0 ± 15.11          | 13.0 ± 41.88  |
| Median                                                                                | -17.9                  | 1.4           |
| Mean difference from Placebo (95% CI)                                                 | -33.97 (-67.54, -0.39) |               |

-Baseline is defined as the last measurement collected before the first dose of double-blind treatment.

Source: Table 14.2-2.1i

During the double-blind Part 1, the percentage changes from baseline in LDL-C were largely consistent across visits, with only isolated outliers. The participant in the placebo group treated with LDLapheresis, showed some visit-to-visit fluctuation. Improvements consistent with the primary efficacy endpoint were seen for absolute change in LDL-C and percent change in ApoB, non-HDL-C, and total cholesterol; the mean between-group differences (95% CI) for change from baseline to Day 330 were -74.64 mg/dL (-150.93, -1.66), -23.03% (-40.13, -5.93), -32.69% (-59.81, -5.56), and -27.79% (-51.88, -3.71), respectively. Inclisiran also decreased VLDL-C; mean (±SD) percent change from baseline at Day 330 was -29.0 (±49.96%) in the inclisiran and 2.0 (±113.44%) in the placebo group. A reduction in Lp(a) was not seen and only small variable effects were observed for triglycerides, HDLC, and Apo A1. Marked reductions in PCSK9 levels indicated effective target engagement with inclisiran; the mean percent change in PCSK9 from baseline to Day 330 was -65.3% in the inclisiran group and -5.1% in the placebo group, with a between-group difference (95% CI) of -60.20% (- 79.76, -40.65). During the open-label Part 2, the mean (±SD) percent change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720 was -12.6 (±28.5%) overall, -8.4 (±31.6%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and -22.2 (±19.9%) in the placeboinclisiran group. In a supplementary analysis excluding the participant on LDL-apheresis (placeboinclisiran group), the mean (±SD) percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720 was -14.1 (±29.23%) overall, -8.4 (±31.61%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and -31.1 (±10.82%) in the placeboinclisiran group. The lower extent of the overall mean change compared to Part 1 and the difference between treatments groups were driven by 2 participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran group who had large increases in LDL-C (+27.3% and +47.4%, respectively). A waterfall plot of percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720 by participant is provided in the figure below. While of variable magnitude, 10/13 participants showed a decrease in LDL-C at Day 720, with the highest change being -50.3% (inclisiran-inclisiran group). In 3/13 participants, LDL-C increased at Day 720, with 2 of the participants showing large increases of +27.3% and +47.4% (see above).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Figure 6: Waterfall plot for LDL-C lowering effect at Day 720- Part 2 Analysis (Open-label phase analysis Set)

<!-- image -->

Source: Figure 14.2-1.1iib

The table below shows changes in LDL-C in the 13 study participants at Day 330 (Part 1 endpoint double-blind period) and Day 720 (Part 2 endpoint open-label period), respectively. In the inclisiraninclisiran group (n=9), 4 participants showed similar or larger decreases in LDL-C at Day 720 compared to Day 330, indicating sustained efficacy over the 2-year treatment period. In addition, 1 participant showed a small reduction in LDL-C at both timepoints, while the remaining 4 participants showed either a smaller decrease or an increase in LDL-C at Day 720 compared to Day 330; these participants also showed larger variations in percentage change in LDL-C between visits in Part 2, with decreases of up to -28.9% at visits other than Day 720. In the placebo-inclisiran group (n=4), 2 participants with increases in LDL-C at Day 330 showed decreases at Day 720; 1 participant who previously showed a decrease in LDL-C at Day 330 more than doubled this decrease at Day 720 and 1 participant treated with LDL-apheresis showed no meaningful change between Day 330 and Day 720. However, the latter participant had reductions in LDL-C of -14.4% to -18.6% between Day 450 and Day 630 before LDL-C increased at Day 720 (+4.5%).

The mean (±SD) absolute change in LDL-C from baseline at Day 720 was -39.8 (±96.59) mg/dL overall, -27.3 (±105.25) mg/dL in the inclisiran-inclisiran and -67.8 (±79.20) mg/dL in the placeboinclisiran group. The mean (±SD) percent changes in ApoB, non-HDL-C, and total cholesterol were overall -7.3% (±29.36) (-3.2% [±32.89] inclisiran-inclisiran, -16.7% [±19.86] placebo-inclisiran), -13.0% (±23.65) (-9.1% [±26.27] inclisiran-inclisiran, -21.8% [±15.75] placebo-inclisiran), and -9.6% (±20.86%) (-7.0% [±23.60] inclisiran-inclisiran, -15.4% [±13.81] placebo-inclisiran), respectively. Supplementary analyses excluding the participant on apheresis showed consistent, though numerically somewhat higher reductions. Consistent lowering of PCSK9 levels was seen with inclisiran across visits; the mean (±SD) change in PCSK9 from baseline to Day 720 was -64.1% (±12.12) overall (-65.0% [±13.25] inclisiran-inclisiran, -62.0% [±10.59] placebo-inclisiran). A supplementary analysis excluding the participant on apheresis showed consistent results. During treatment with inclisiran, all participants showed consistent lowering of PCSK9 across all visits. The mean (±SD) percent changes in Lp(a) and VLDL-C from baseline at Day 720 overall were -8.8% (±22.03) (-8.8% [±19.16] inclisiran-inclisiran, -8.6% [±31.02] placebo-inclisiran) and -1.4% (±69.03) (+4.9% [±77.47] inclisiran-inclisiran, -15.6% [±51.66] placebo-inclisiran), respectively. Overall, the mean percent changes in HDL-C and ApoA1 from baseline at Day 720 were +13.9% (±18.81) (+9.1 [±12.49] inclisiran-inclisiran, +24.9% [±27.72] placebo-inclisiran) and +6.5% (±10.10) (+2.5% [±9.31] inclisiran-inclisiran, +15.5% [±4.39] placebo-inclisiran), respectively. Only a small, variable effect was seen for triglycerides.

In line with the primary endpoint, the mean time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C (after Day 90 up to Day 330) was -21.0% in the inclisiran and 13.0% in the placebo group (between-group difference [95% CI] -33.97% [-67.54, -0.39]). A supplementary analysis excluding the participant on apheresis showed consistent results. The percentage changes from baseline in LDL-C in Part 1were largely consistent across visits, with isolated outliers; the participant in the placebo group treated with LDLapheresis showed some visit-to-visit fluctuation. Reductions were seen for absolute change in LDL-C and percent change in ApoB, non-HDL-C, VLDL-C, and total cholesterol. No reduction was seen in Lp(a) and only small variable effects for triglycerides, HDL-C, and Apo A1. As regards Part 2, there was a lower extent of the overall mean change in LDL-C compared to Part 1; the difference between treatments groups was driven by 2 participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran group who had increases in LDL-C of +27.3% and +47.4%, respectively. A waterfall plot of percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720 showed a variable magnitude; 10/13 participants had a decrease in LDL-C at Day 720, while in 3/13 participants, LDL-C increased at Day 720. In a comparison of changes in LDL-C between Day 330 (Part 1) and Day 720 (Part 2), 4 participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran group (n=9) showed similar or larger decreases in LDL-C at Day 720 compared to Day 330, 1 participant showed a small reduction in LDL-C at both timepoints, and 4 participants showed either a smaller decrease or an increase in LDL-C at Day 720 compared to Day 330. In the placebo-inclisiran group (n=4), 2 participants with increases in LDL-C at Day 330 showed decreases at Day 720, 1 participant with a decrease in LDL-C at Day 330 more than double of this decrease at Day 720, and 1 participant on LDLapheresis no meaningful change between Day 330 and Day 720.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Ancillary analyses

## Exploratory endpoints

As regards individual responsiveness of participants, during the double-blind period 6/9 (66.7%) participants in the inclisiran vs. 1/4 (25.0%) in the placebo group achieved a ˃15% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330, and 5/9 (55.6%) in the inclisiran vs. no participant in the placebo group achieved a ≥ 20% reduction. A ≥30% reduction in LDL-C was achieved by 3/9 (33.3%) inclisirantreated participants. A more than 15% LDL-C reduction was described by the 2023 European Atherosclerosis Society (EAS) consensus statement update on HoFH as being a clinically relevant improvement with a PCSK9-directed therapy (Cuchel et al 2023). However, consistent with the high mean baseline LDL-C levels (inclisiran: 265.0 mg/dL, placebo: 288.3 mg/dL), only 1 participant in the inclisiran group reached the pre-specified LDL-C threshold of &lt;130 mg/dL and no participant a LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. Results are summarised in the table below.

| Number of participants with > 15% reduction in LDL-C from BL | 6/9 (66.7)               | 1/4 (25.0)            |
| Number of participants with ≥ 20% reduction in LDL-C from BL | 5/9 (55.6)               | 0                     |
| Number of participants reaching LDL-C < 130 mg/dL            | 1/9 (11.1)               | 0                     |
| Number of participants reaching LDL-C < 100 mg/dL            | 0                        | 0                     |

During Part 2, 7/13 (53.8%) participants achieved a ˃15% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720, 4/9 (44.4%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 3/4 (75.0%) in the placebo-inclisiran group. A ≥20% reduction in LDL-C was achieved by 6/13 participants (46.2%), 4/9 (44.4%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 2/4 (50.0%) in the placebo-inclisiran group. A ≥30% reduction in LDL-C was seen in 4/13 (30.8%) participants, 2/9 (22.2%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 2/4 (50.0%) in the placebo-inclisiran group. Only 2 participants reached the pre-specified LDL-C threshold of &lt;130 mg/dL (1 participant per group) and 1 participant (inclisiran-inclisiran group) achieved an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. No participant reached an LDL-C below 70 mg/dL.

An exploratory analysis of LDL-C response by genotype included only participants with adjudicated central laboratory genetic results (n=12/13). During Part 1, consistent LDL-C lowering was seen with inclisiran across the included genetic variant groups. For the 2 main genotypes, compound heterozygous LDLR defective/defective and compound heterozygous LDLR null/defective, the LDL-C reductions with inclisiran were -20.5% (n=4) and -18.1% (n=4), respectively. For the open-label Part 2, a subgroup analysis of percent changes in LDL-C from baseline at Day 720 by genotype was not specified in the protocol as an exploratory endpoint but was added for completeness in the SAP. For the compound heterozygous LDLR defective/defective subgroup (n=5), the mean (±SD) change in LDL-C overall was -20.7% (±19.79) (-21.2% [±22.81] inclisiran-inclisiran [n=4], -18.9% placebo-inclisiran group [n=1]). For the compound heterozygous LDLR null/defective subgroup (n=5), the mean (±SD) change in LDL-C was +2.9% (±36.21) overall (+2.5% [±41.79] inclisiran-inclisiran [n=4], +4.5% placebo-inclisiran [n=1]); changes in LDL-C ranged from -42.1% to +47.4% and the 2 participants with large increases in LDL-C (see also above) are both part of this subgroup ( 014, 009).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Carotid intima media thickness (cIMT) was measured as an exploratory endpoint at Day 1 (baseline), Day 330 (Year 1), and Day 720 (Year 2) by ultrasonography, using local machines; right and left common carotid arteries were assessed. No effect on changes from baseline to Day 330 in cIMT was seen in either treatment group. The mean (±SD) baseline cIMT for the average of right and left cIMT was 0.492 mm (±0.097) in the inclisiran and 0.486 mm (±0.070) in the placebo group. At Day 330, the mean (±SD) changes from baseline were -0.005 mm ±0.067 in the inclisiran and -0.033 mm (±0.034) in placebo group. At Day 720 (Part 2), the mean (±SD) change from baseline was 0.053 mm (±0.112) overall, 0.049 mm (±0.098) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 0.063 mm (±0.176) in the placebo-inclisiran group.

As regards frequency of LDL-apheresis, only 1/13 (7.7%) participant in the placebo-inclisiran group (1/4; 25%) was treated with LDL-apheresis; thus no relevant analysis is feasible. The frequency of LDL-apheresis for this participant remained unchanged at \"every 2 weeks\" over the course of the study, except for occasionally longer periods caused by exceptional circumstances (e.g., vacation).

## Pharmacokinetic results

An exploratory endpoint for this study was the evaluation of inclisiran concentrations. For pharmacokinetic results, see section Pharmacokinetics above.

As regards exploratory results, 6/9 (66.7%) participants in the inclisiran versus 1/4 (25.0%) in the placebo group achieved a ˃15% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330, 5/9 (55.6%) versus none, respectively a ≥20%, and 3/9 (33.3%) versus none a ≥30% reduction. However, only 1 participant in the inclisiran group reached the pre-specified LDL-C threshold of &lt;130 mg/dL and none an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. During Part 2, 7/13 (53.8%) participants achieved a ˃15% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720, 6/13 (46.2%) a ≥20%, and 4/13 (30.8%) a ≥30% reduction. Only 2 participants reached the pre-specified LDL-C threshold of &lt;130 mg/dL, 1 participant an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL, and no participant an LDL-C below 70 mg/dL. Consistent LDL-C lowering was seen across the included genetic variants. No clinically relevant effect of inclisiran on carotid intima media thickness (cIMT) was seen. Since only 1 participant was treated with LDL-apheresis, no relevant analysis is feasible in this regard.

ORION-16 (CKJX839C12301, EudraCT No.: 2020-002757-18): \"Two part (double-blind inclisiran versus placebo [Year 1] followed by openlabel inclisiran [Year 2]) randomised multicenter study to evaluate safety, tolerability, and efficacy of inclisiran in adolescents (12 to less than 18 years) with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia and elevated LDLcholesterol\"

Study ORION-16 evaluated safety, tolerability, and efficacy of inclisiran in adolescents from 12 to less than 18 years of age with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C). It is part of the inclisiran paediatric program as agreed with European Medicines Agency (EMA) in the paediatric investigation plan (PIP; EMEA-002214-PIP01-17-M02, P/0322/2023) and intended to support the registration of inclisiran in adolescents with HeFH. Study ORION-16 also included pharmacokinetic (PK) and pharmacogenetic assessments; PK assessment was based on sparse PK sampling (i.e. 3 samples at the Day 1 visit; pre-dose immediately before the first administration of study drug, 4-, and 24-hours after first dose; see section Pharmacokinetics.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The study was conducted in two-parts, a double-blind, inclisiran versus placebo part (Year 1) followed by an open-label inclisiran part (Year 2). It was a randomised, multicentre Phase III study in adolescents aged 12 to &lt;18 years with HeFH and elevated LDL-C (&gt;130 mg/dL; 3.4 mmol/L) on stable, individualised, optimal standard of care (SoC) background lipid-lowering therapy including maximally tolerated statin treatment, at the Investigator's discretion.

According to the data provided, study ORION-16 was conducted in compliance with Good Clinical Practice (GCP). The study design is summarised in the following table.

## Table 21: Summary of study design

The two-part, randomised, multicentre, international Phase III study had two sequential parts:

- Part 1/Year 1: 12 months double-blind, parallel group period, in which participants were randomised in a 2:1 ratio to receive either inclisiran sodium 300 mg s.c. or placebo. The primary endpoint was assessed at Day 330.
- Part 2 / Year 2: 12 months single-arm, open-label follow-up period, with all participants receiving inclisiran sodium 300 mg s.c.
* Primary endpoint assessed at Day 330.
- # \"Switch Day\": Participants randomised to placebo in Part 1 started to receive inclisiran on Day 360. Participants randomised to inclisiran in Part 1 received placebo on Day 360 only to maintain the blind for Part 1. [Study ORION-16 Figure 9-1]

ORION-16 Study Design

<!-- image -->

The completed paediatric study ORION-16 (CKJX839C12301) is part of post-authorisation measures for Leqvio (inclisiran) and was intended to investigate the use of inclisiran in adolescents from 12 to less than 18 years of age with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) and elevated LDLcholesterol. Currently, inclisiran is only licensed in adults, including patients with HeFH, from 18 years of age onwards. The study has been submitted in accordance with Article 46 of Regulation (EC) No1901/2006, as amended.

Study ORION-16 was conducted as a two-part, multicentre, randomised study; Part 1 (year 1) investigated inclisiran versus placebo in a double-blind setting, while in Part 2 (year2) all participants received open-label inclisiran.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Methods

## Study participants

The study population consisted of adolescents (male or female; 12 to &lt;18 years) with a genetic or clinical diagnosis of HeFH and LDL-C &gt;130 mg/dL (3.4 mmol/L) and being on a maximally tolerated dose of statin (at investigator's discretion) with or without other lipid lowering therapy (e.g., ezetimibe).

Key inclusion criteria were:

- HeFH diagnosed either by genetic testing or on phenotypic criteria (according to the EAS Consensus Panel),
- fasting LDL-C &gt;130 mg/dL (3.4 mmol/L) at screening,
- on maximally tolerated dose of statin (Investigator's discretion) with or without other lipidlowering therapy (e.g. ezetimibe),
- participants on lipid-lowering therapies (such as statin and/or e.g. ezetimibe) should have been on a stable dose for ≥ 30 days before screening with no planned medication or dose changes during study participation,
- fasting triglycerides &lt;400 mg/dL (4.5 mmol/L) at screening, and
- eGFR &gt;30 mL/min/1.73 m2 at screening.

Key exclusion criteria were:

- previous treatment (within 90 days of screening) with monoclonal antibodies directed towards PCSK9,
- active liver disease or unexplained, confirmed ALT, AST elevation &gt;3x ULN, or total bilirubin elevation &gt;2x ULN (except patients with Gilbert's syndrome),
- pregnant or nursing females,
- HoFH,
- recent and/or planned use of other investigational medicinal products or devices,
- secondary hypercholesterolemia, e.g. hypothyroidism or nephrotic syndrome, and
- major adverse cardiovascular events within 3 months prior to randomization.

The study included male and female adolescents from 12 to less than 18 years of age with a genetic or clinical diagnosis of HeFH and an LDL-C &gt;130 mg/dL who were on a maximally tolerated dose of an HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor (statin) with or without other lipid lowering therapy (excluding PCSK9 inhibitors). Patients with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HoFH) or secondary hypercholesterolemia were excluded.

## Treatments

During Part 1 (year 1) participants received either investigational drug inclisiran sodium 300 mg (equivalent to 284 mg inclisiran) in 1.5 ml solution (batch numbers 2040329, 2040834, 2040921, 2046470) or reference drug placebo formulation to this active drug formulation (batch numbers 2040833, 2041156, 2041335, 2043088) for s.c. injection.

In Part 2 (year 2) all participants received Inclisiran sodium 300 mg (batch numbers 2040329, 2040979, 2041729, 2047184).

During Part 1, participants received either inclisiran at the licensed standard dose and posology for adults or placebo. During Part 2, all participants received standard dose inclisiran.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Objectives / outcomes / endpoints

During the double-blind Part 1 (year 1) of the study, the primary efficacy and safety data comparing inclisiran to placebo were collected. The open-label single arm Part 2 (year 2) of the study allowed the collection of longer-term data on inclisiran, with a focus on longer-term safety.

Objectives and endpoints of study ORION-16 are summarised in the table below.

|-------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| • Explore the effect of inclisiran compared to placebo on the response of LDL-C reduction by underlying causal mutations of HeFH at Day 330 (Year 1) • Explore the effect of inclisiran compared to placebo (at Day 330) and long-term (at Day 720) on the carotid intima- media thickness (cIMT) • Explore (sparse) PK of inclisiran in adolescents with HeFH (subgroup of 12 - 15 participants) | • Percentage change and absolute change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 (Year 1), by type of underlying causal mutation • Change in cIMT from baseline to Day 330 (Year 1) and Day 720 (Year 2) • Evaluation of inclisiran concentrations at 4 and 24 hours after the first dose |

For the primary efficacy assessment reflexive LDL-C was used for analysis, i.e., Friedewald formula to calculate LDL-C levels in a first step, and in those cases where the formula is not valid (i.e., triglycerides &gt;400 mg/dL, calculated LDL &lt;40 mg/dL), in a second step, ultracentrifugation to measure LDL-C directly.

Participants were in a fasted state for all efficacy laboratory assessments. From randomisation (Day 1) until Day 360, the results were blinded to participants, investigator staff, persons performing the assessments, and the clinical trial team, except for bioanalytical analysis related to PK which was conducted in an unblinded manner.

In addition, carotid intima-media thickness (cIMT) was measured locally by ultrasonography and was exploratory / hypothesis-generating in nature.

All participants were invited to optional pharmacogenetic analyses of the underlying causal mutation(s) of their HeFH. Samples were analysed for potential mutations in the LDL receptor (LDLR), ApoB, PCSK9, and LDLR adaptor protein 1 (LDLRAP1) genes.

The key safety assessments consisted of monitoring and recording of all adverse events (AEs), laboratory evaluations (haematology, biochemistry, urinalysis, pregnancy tests), anti-drug antibody (ADA) measurement, vital signs, growth (height, weight, body mass index [BMI]), and pubertal development (sex hormones, Tanner staging). Other safety evaluations were physical examinations, neurological evaluations, and electrocardiograms (ECGs).

The primary endpoint was the percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to the end of the doubleblind treatment period at day 330 (year 1).

Secondary endpoints included absolute change in LDL-C and other lipid parameters during Part 1 as well as assessments from baseline to the end of the open-label Part 2, i.e. day 720.

Exploratory endpoints included individual responsiveness of participants, changes in LDL-C by type of underlying causal mutation, change in carotid intima-media thickness (cIMT), and evaluation of inclisiran pharmacokinetics (PK) based on sparse data.

The endpoints are considered to be in line with previous adult inclisiran studies.

## Sample size

Sample size was calculated based on detecting a reduction of at least 30% in the inclisiran compared to the placebo group in percent change of LDL-C from baseline to Day 330. The primary efficacy variable was assumed to have a standard deviation of 30% in the control group and 20% in the inclisiran group. These assumptions were based on observed results from adult inclisiran studies.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The initial planned sample size was approximately 150 adolescents; this was adjusted to at least 102 participants in protocol amendment 1 dated 28-Oct-2022. A sample size of at least 102 participants provided more than 90% power to detect a 30% reduction at significance level of 0.025 (one-sided test) in a 2:1 (inclisiran vs. placebo) randomisation ratio.

The initial planned sample size of 150 adolescents was significantly reduced to at least 102 participants in a protocol amendment in October 2022. A sample size of ≥102 participants allowed only for detecting a ≥30% reduction in LDL-C in the chosen setting.

## Randomisation and blinding (masking)

Part 1 of the study was double-blind and placebo-controlled. Participants, investigator staff, persons performing the assessments, and the clinical trial team were blinded to the identity of the treatment from the time of randomisation until at least database lock for all Part 1 (year 1) data. Exceptions were the independent statistician and programmer to prepare interim analysis reports for the DMC, and the PK analyst to avoid unnecessary analysis of placebo samples; in addition, a PK interim analysis (IA) team had access to unblinded individual-level PK/baseline data in order to update a PK extrapolation to paediatrics before the Part 1 database lock to support early planning of a dose selection strategy for paediatric studies in younger children. Part 2 of the study had a single-arm, open-label design.

After baseline visit (Day 1), participants were randomised by interactive response technology in a 2:1 ratio to receive double-blind s.c. injections of inclisiran sodium 300 mg or placebo in Part 1 (year 1) of the study (days 1, 90, 270). On day 360 (\"switch day\"), participants randomised to placebo in Part 1 received inclisiran and participants randomised to inclisiran in Part 1 received placebo. This dose remained blinded in order to maintain the blind for Part 1 of the study. Subsequently, all participants received open-label s.c. injections of inclisiran sodium 300 mg in Part 2 (year 2).

Investigational and control drugs were supplied by the MAH as single-use pre-filled syringes for s.c. injection. Placebo was supplied as sterile normal saline (0.9% sodium chloride in water for injection) and administered as a 1.5 mL s.c. injection to match the dose in the inclisiran arm. The placebo prefilled syringes were blinded and were identical in appearance to their active counterparts.

Part 1 was conducted randomised, double-blind, and placebo-controlled. In Part 2, all participants received open-label inclisiran.

## Statistical methods

Efficacy and safety analyses of Part 1 data were performed after all participants completed the Day 360 visit or discontinued prior to the Day 360 visit; analyses of Part 2 data were performed after all participants completed the Day 720 (EoS) visit or discontinued prior to the Day 720 visit. In cases where the dosing time at Day 360 did not allow to determine whether data belonged to Part 1 or 2, the data were considered to belong to Part 1.

The following data sets were analysed. The Screened Set (SCR) included all participants who provided study informed consent. The Randomised Set (RAN) consisted of all participants who received a randomisation number, regardless of whether double-blind treatment was received or not. The Full Analysis Set (FAS) consisted of all randomised participants with the exception of those mis-randomised participants who did not receive study drug; mis-randomised participants were defined as those who were not qualified for randomization but were inadvertently randomised into the study. According to the intent to treat principle, participants were analysed according to the treatment they were assigned to during the randomisation procedure. The FAS was used in analyses for the primary, secondary, and exploratory efficacy objectives in Part 1 of the study. The Safety Set (SAF) included all participants who received at least 1 dose of study treatment. Participants were analysed according to the study treatment received. This was the primary population for the safety analyses in Part 1. The SAF allowed the inclusion of nonrandomised participants who received double-blind treatment in error. The PK analysis Set included all randomised participants who had at least 1 PK measurement. Participants were analysed according to the treatment they received. This was the primary population for the PK analyses. The Open-label Phase Analysis Set (OAS) included all participants who entered Part 2 of the study and received at least 1 dose of study treatment in Part 2. The OAS was used to analyse data from participants who entered Part 2.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

For the primary endpoint percentage change in LDL-C from pre-dose baseline measurement to Day 330, the primary null hypothesis (H0) was the difference (inclisiran minus placebo) in the LS mean percentage change from baseline to the Day 330 visit in LDL-C= 0 and the alternative hypothesis (Ha) the difference in the LS mean percentage change from baseline to the Day 330 visit in LDL-C &lt; 0. The primary objective would be achieved if the null hypothesis is rejected at a significance level of 0.025 (one-sided test). The primary endpoint was analysed using an analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) model, in which the response variable was the LDL-C percentage change from baseline to Day 330. The model included the fixed effects of treatment group and baseline age group (≥12-&lt;15 or ≥15-&lt;18 years of age), with baseline LDL-C as a covariate. No remaining intercurrent events were expected. Multiple imputation was used to impute missing data based on a washout model which is a modified controlbased pattern-mixture-model. The wash-out model imputed the missing values for participants in the inclisiran group with data from participants with similar background characteristics in the placebo group, under the assumption of missing not at random (MNAR) unless the participants with missing values completed the first 3 doses and had data available after Day 330. For participants who completed the first 3 doses of inclisiran and had data available after Day 330, and participants in the placebo group (regardless of how many doses taken), the missing values were imputed under the assumption of missing at random (MAR). A total of 100 imputed datasets were created. The ANCOVA model was fit to each imputed dataset, and results were combined using Rubin's combination rules. For sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoint a linear mixed-effect model for repeated measures (MMRM) analysis without multiple imputation (under an implicit assumption of MAR), a MMRM analysis on the imputed datasets, and multiple imputation will be based on a control-based pattern mixture model (under the assumption of MNAR), and an ANCOVA using the washout model including the following fixed effects: baseline concomitant lipid-modifying therapies, baseline age group, region, and a treatment-by region interaction were performed. Supplementary analyses included LDL-C percent change from baseline to Day 330 by subgroups gender, age group (≥12 to &lt;15, ≥15 to &lt;18 years of age), race, ethnicity, BMI, region, baseline statin use, baseline LDL-C, and baseline PCSK9. The MMRM model was used to evaluate the difference between inclisiran and placebo groups in each subgroup.

The key secondary endpoints time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330 (year 1), defined as the average of percent changes from baseline to Days 150, 270, and 330 and absolute change in LDL-C and percentage change in Apo B, Lp(a), non-HDL-C, and total cholesterol from baseline to Day 330 (year 1) were analysed using the same ANCOVA with multiple imputation using wash-out model, except the time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330; the latter was analysed using a MMRM model with a controlbased pattern mixture model for missing value imputation. A hierarchical testing strategy was applied to control the family-wise type I error rate at the one-sided significance level of alpha = 0.025 to assess the superiority of inclisiran over placebo; hypotheses were tested in the order listed above. For sensitivity analysis a MMRM analysis without multiple imputation that assumed missing data are MAR

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

was performed and MMRM analysis using control-based pattern mixture model for missing value imputation was performed for other key secondary endpoints. Supplementary analyses of the key secondary endpoints were performed in subgroups as specified for the primary analysis. The MMRM model (using control-based pattern mixture model for missing value imputation) was used to evaluate the difference between inclisiran and placebo groups in each subgroup. Another supplementary analysis for Lp(a) was applied in log-transformed scale using the same MMRM without multiple imputation specified above.

Other secondary efficacy endpoints included the percent change and absolute change from baseline throughout the time course up to Day 720 (year 2) of LDL-C, Apo B, Lp(a), non-HDL-C, total cholesterol, triglycerides, HDL-C, VLDL-C, Apo A1, and PCSK9. Descriptive summaries by treatment group were provided for these endpoints.

For exploratory variables LDL-C &lt;50 mg/dL, &lt;70 mg/dL, &lt;100 mg/dL, and &lt;130 mg/dL, as well as ≥50% LDL-C reduction from baseline, frequencies and percentages of participants were summarised by treatment group at Day 330 (year 1) and Day 720 (year 2). In addition, reductions of ≥20% and ≥30% LDL-C from baseline were summarised at Day 720 (year 2) as supportive analyses. Summary statistics for LDL-C percent and absolute change from baseline were provided by treatment group in subgroups defined by underlying genetic mutation(s). Furthermore, values of cIMT were summarised at each time point by treatment group. Change in cIMT from baseline to Day 330 (year 1) and Day 720 (year 2) were summarised by treatment group.

As regards PK analyses, inclisiran plasma concentration data were listed by treatment, participant, and sampling time point. Descriptive summary statistics were provided by sampling time point, including the frequency (n, %) of concentrations below the LLOQ; these were reported as zero. Summary descriptive statistics included mean (arithmetic and geometric), standard deviation (SD), coefficient of variation (CV; arithmetic and geometric), median, minimum, and maximum. An interim analysis (IA) on PK data was performed to update a PK extrapolation to paediatrics (i.e., to further refine the extrapolation from adult PK data with adolescent PK data) as part of developing a dose selection strategy for planned future paediatric studies in younger children &lt;12 years of age.

As regards safety, the number and percentage of participants who had treatment-emergent adverse events (AEs), treatment-emergent serious AEs (SAEs), injection site reactions, and treatmentemergent events related to other safety topics (including cardiac, hepatic, hypersensitivity, musculoskeletal, neurological, renal, psychiatric, new onset or worsening of diabetes) were summarised. For laboratory data and vital signs, absolute values and change from baseline were summarised. The number and percentage of participants with categorical criteria were summarised. In addition, the number and percentage of participants with newly occurring notable abnormalities for selected laboratory parameters and vital signs were summarised. The number and percentage of participants with treatment-induced transient and persistent anti-drug antibodies (ADA) and no treatment-induced ADA were summarised. Shift from baseline in Tanner stage was summarised by sex, treatment, and visit. Shift in menarche status from baseline was summarised by visit and treatment group. Subgroup analyses by gender, age group (≥12 to &lt;15 and ≥15 to &lt;18 years of age), race, and BMI were performed for all treatment-emergent AEs and separately for injection site reactions. IA for safety monitoring were planned at regular intervals. IA before unblinding for the analysis of Part 1 of the study were performed by an independent statistician/programmer not involved in the trial conduct. The interim results were reviewed by the independent data monitoring committee (DMC).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The CHMP concluded that the statistical methods applied were adequate.

## Results

## Participant flow, numbers analysed, and recruitment

Study ORION-16 was started on 27 January 2021 (first participant first visit) and completed on 27 November 2024 (last participant last visit); the CSR version 1.0 is dated 14 March 2025.

A total of 141 participants were enrolled, 93 in the inclisiran and 48 in the placebo group. The were randomised from 51 study centres in 26 countries (Argentina 1, Brazil 3, Canada 1, Czech Republic 2, France 3, Germany 2, Greece 1, Hungary 1, Israel 2, Italy 4, Jordan 1, Lebanon 1, Malaysia 1, Netherlands 2, Norway 1, Poland 2, Russian Federation 2, Slovakia 1, Slovenia 1, South Africa 3, Spain 4, Switzerland 1, Taiwan 1, Turkey 4, United Kingdom 1, United States of America 5).

Of the 141 randomised participants, 98.6% (139/141) completed Part 1, 97.8% (91/93) in the inclisiran and 100% (48/48) in the placebo group. The reasons for the two discontinuations were participant/guardian decision (change of mind regarding study participation) and physician decision (following discontinuation of study drug due to mild AEs of nausea and vertigo). All 139 participants who completed Part 1 entered and completed Part 2, i.e., 91 participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 48 in the placebo-inclisiran group.

Of the 141 participants randomised, 139 completed Part 1 and entered and completed Part 2.

## Conduct of the study

The study protocol was amended twice. Key features of each amendment are given in the table below. There were no other changes to the study conduct.

Table 23: Protocol amendments

| Version and date         | Summary of key changes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
|--------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Amendment 1, 28-Oct-2022 | To reduce the sample size of the study from approximately 150 participants to a minimum of 102 participants; the revised sample size was based on a review of existing evidence. The revised sample size of at least 102 participants provides more than 90% power to assess the primary efficacy endpoint, under conservative assumptions of a 30% between-group difference and standard deviations of 30/40% for the inclisiran and placebo groups. However, the actual enrolment was 141 participants, which increased the statistical power for the primary efficacy endpoint and contributed additional safety data. |
| Amendment 2, 17-Feb-2023 | To add an interim analysis for the PK data collected on Day 1, to update a PK extrapolation to paediatrics, i.e., to further refine the extrapolation from adult PK data with adolescent PK data, covering a lower range of body weights and ages. The updated PK extrapolation was critical as part of developing a dose selection strategy for planned future paediatric studies in younger children (<12 years). The PK IA was performed by a separate team not involved in any other study activities before the Part 1 (year 1) database lock, when the study was unblinded as per protocol.                         |

As regards changes in planned analyses, after the Part 1 (year 1) database lock, an analysis of percentage change in PCSK9 from baseline to Day 330 using an ANCOVA model was added with treatment as fixed effect and baseline PCSK9 and baseline age group as covariates; only descriptive summary statistics by treatment and visit was pre-specified for PCSK9.

After the Part 2 (year 2) database lock, an implausible increase in Lp(a) at Day 720 was observed for participant C12301- 013 (inclisiran-inclisiran group), which had a large impact on the mean percent change from baseline for this parameter. The Lp(a) value for this participant was 17 nmol/L at Day 1 and consistently low at subsequent visits but markedly higher (337 nmol/L) at Day 720, corresponding to an 1882.4% increase from baseline (Listing 16.2.6-1.2ii). This result was not plausible given that Lp(a) levels are largely genetically determined. An analytical error at the central laboratory was excluded as a potential reason for the unusual value at Day 720; however, it was noticed that participant C12301- 014 (inclisiran-inclisiran group), who had the Day 720 visit at the same investigational site on the same day as participant C12301- 013, showed an atypical decrease in Lp(a) at Day 720. The Lp(a) value for this participant was 355 nmol/L at Day 1 and consistently high at subsequent visits but only 16 nmol/L at Day 720 (Listing 16.2.6-1.2ii). Hence, the most plausible explanation for the observed outlier values at Day 720 is a sample mix-up between the two participants C12301- 013 and C12301- 014. There is no evidence to suggest sample mix-ups at any other time points for these two participants. A post-hoc sensitivity analysis, excluding participants C12301- 013 and C12301- 014 from the OAS, was therefore performed. As a conservative approach, absolute and percent mean changes from baseline at Day 720 were analysed for all lipid/lipoprotein secondary endpoint parameters, i.e., Lp(a), LDL-C, Apo B, non-HDL-C, total cholesterol, HDL-C, ApoA1, VLDL-C, and triglycerides. For all other lipid/lipoprotein parameters, including LDL-C, results of the sensitivity analysis were consistent with the primary analysis, with no clinically meaningful differences.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

With amendment 1 in October 2022, the sample size of the study was significantly reduced (from 150 to ≥102 participants). Nevertheless, 141 participants were enrolled. Amendment 2 in February 2023 added an interim analysis for the PK data collected on Day 1. The changes in the planned conduct of the study are not considered to have influenced the outcome of study ORION-16.

## Baseline data

Overall, demographic and baseline characteristics were comparable between the treatment groups. Approximately half of the randomised participants were female (53.2%; 75/141) and the mean age was 15.1 years (range 12 to 17 years). The majority of participants (90.8%; 128/141) were white, with all participants of other races being in the inclisiran group. The mean (±SD) BMI was 22.7 ±4.88 kg/m 2 . Mean (±SD) baseline LDL-C and PCSK9 levels of study participants were 183.1 ±48.67 mg/dL and 376.4 ±157.39 ng/mL, respectively. Overall, 92.9% (131/141) of participants (96.8% inclisiran, 85.4% placebo) received statin treatment. About one fourth of participants (24.8%; 35/141) were treated with other lipid-lowering treatments, mostly ezetimibe (23.4%; 33/141), with a similar proportion in both groups. All participants had HeFH, diagnosed either clinically by EAS Consensus Panel criteria or clinically and genetically; the majority of participants (95.7%, 135/141) had a genetic diagnosis of HeFH, including LDLR null (negative) mutations (51.1%; 72/141) and LDLR defective mutations (39.7%; 56/141) as the most prevalent variants. There were no important differences in genotypes between treatment groups.

In Part 1, 12.1% (17/141) of participants reported at least one protocol deviation, 16.1% (15/93) in the inclisiran and 4.2% (2/48) in the placebo group. The most common protocol deviations (&gt;6 participants) were PK sample(s) being taken either before a separate consent signature to the main informed consent form was provided or without a separate informed consent; samples without consent were not used. Three (3) protocol deviations were related to changes in concomitant lipid-lowering treatment, a change from pravastatin to ezetimibe during the screening period, a prolonged interruption of atorvastatin and ezetimibe before Day 330 visit in the inclisiran, and an increase of the rosuvastatin dose in the placebo group. In Part 2, 1.4% (2/139) of participants reported at least one protocol deviation, all (2.2%; 2/91) in the inclisiran-inclisiran group. The first protocol deviation was receipt of study treatment twice within &lt;60 days (i.e., 53 days), which did not cause any AEs and the second concerned accidental unblinding by site in interactive response technology of the Part 1 treatment assignment at the EoS/Day 720 visit.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

During Part 1, the mean duration on study and of exposure to study treatment were 357.9 days in the inclisiran and 362.9 days in the placebo group. For Part 2, the mean duration on study and the mean duration of exposure to study treatment were 361.1 days and 360.6 days, respectively, with similar durations in the inclisiran-inclisiran and placebo-inclisiran groups. Over the entire study duration (Part 1 and 2), 97.9% (138/141) of participants were administered all 6 doses of study drug. The mean duration on study was 714.6 days and the mean duration of exposure to study treatment 714.1 days, with similar durations in the inclisiran-inclisiran and placebo-inclisiran groups. The overall patient years of exposure to study treatment was 275.6, 180.5 in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 95.1 in the placebo inclisiran group.

Demographic and baseline characteristics were comparable between treatment groups. Approximately half of the participants were female and the mean age was 15.1 years (range 12 to 17 years). The majority of participants had a genetic diagnosis of HeFH and there were no important differences in genotypes between treatment groups.

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary analysis

The LDL-C LS mean percent change (95% CI) from baseline to Day 330 was -27.14% (-32.04, -22.24) in the inclisiran and 1.40% (-3.97, 6.78) in the placebo group, with a between-group difference (95% CI) of -28.54% (-35.81, -21.27; p&lt;0.0001). The study met its primary objective of demonstrating superiority of inclisiran compared to placebo in reducing LDL-C in adolescents with HeFH; it showed a clinically relevant and statistically significant reduction in LDL C with inclisiran.

Table 24: Primary Efficacy Endpoint - Percent Change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 (Part 1, Full Analysis Set)

| Inclisiran N=93 PCHG LS Mean (95% - CI)   | Placebo N=48 PCHG LS Mean (95% - CI)   | Difference between Inclisiran and Placebo - LS Mean (95% CI)   | Difference between Inclisiran and Placebo - One-sided p-value   |
|-------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|
| -27.14 (-32.04, -22.24)                   | 1.40 (-3.97, 6.78)                     | -28.54 (-35.81, -21.27)                                        | <.0001                                                          |

Source: [Study ORION-16 - Table 14.2-1.1i]

The primary analysis used a pre-specified multiple imputation washout model/ANCOVA. Three (3) prespecified sensitivity analyses with respect to handling missing data were performed, a linear-MMRM without multiple imputation, a MMRM analysis using a control-based pattern mixture model (PMM), and ANCOVA using the multiple imputation washout model including the fixed effects baseline concomitant lipid-modifying therapies, baseline age group, region, and treatment by-region interaction. Results were consistent with the primary analysis independent of the sensitivity analysis used. The between-group differences (95% CI) for LS mean change in LDL-C were -29.10% (-36.88, -21.32), -28.69% (-36.44, -20.93), and -32.15% (-42.42, -21.87), respectively (all p-values &lt;0.0001).

Subgroup analyses by age group, gender, race, ethnicity, BMI, region, baseline statin use, baseline LDL-C, and PCSK9 showed clinically relevant and nominally statistically significant placebo-adjusted percentage changes in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 except for region = other. In addition, consistent reductions in LDL-C were seen across subgroups in the inclisiran group, while some larger variability was observed with placebo, in line with the smaller numbers in this group. The non-White race subgroups had few participants only in the inclisiran group and none in the placebo group, thus preventing the pre-specified model-based analysis. Similarly, the ethnicity subgroup \"other\" consisted of only 3 participants and the model-based analysis could therefore not be performed.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Study ORION-16 met the primary objective of demonstrating superiority of inclisiran compared to placebo in reducing LDL-C in adolescents with HeFH on maximally tolerated statin treatment. The effect of a between-group difference (95% CI) of -28.54% (-35.81, -21.27; p&lt;0.0001) between inclisiran and placebo is considered clinically relevant. Results of sensitivity and subgroup analyses were consistent with the primary analysis.

## Secondary analyses

Concerning key secondary efficacy endpoints, relevant improvements were seen for time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330, consistent with the primary efficacy endpoint. The LS mean percent change was -26.04% in the inclisiran and 3.26% in the placebo group, with a between-group difference (95% CI) of -29.30% (-36.24, -22.36; p&lt;0.0001).

Table 25: Key secondary efficacy endpoint - Time adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330 (Part 1, Full Analysis Set)

| Inclisiran N=93 PCHG LS Mean (95% CI)   | Placebo N=48 PCHG LS Mean (95% - CI)   | Difference between Inclisiran and Placebo - LS Mean (95% CI)   | Difference between Inclisiran and Placebo - One-sided p- value   |
|-----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------|
| -26.04 (-30.11, -21.98)                 | 3.26 (-2.37, 8.89)                     | -29.30 (-36.24, -22.36)                                        | <.0001                                                           |

* A control-based PMM was used for missing data imputation with 100 total imputed datasets. A MMRM on each of the 100 datasets was performed by including fixed effects for treatment, visit (Day 150, 270, and 330), interaction between treatment and visit, and baseline LDL-C and baseline age group as a covariate. A linear combination of the estimated means after Day 90 and up to Day 330 was used to compare treatment groups. Treatment effects from the 100 analyses were combined using Rubin's method.

Source: [Study ORION-16 - Table 14.2-2.1i]

Relevant improvements were also seen for absolute change in LDL-C, and percent change in Apo B, non-HDL-C, and total cholesterol; the LS mean between-group differences (95% CI) for change from baseline to Day 330 were -49.99 mg/dL (-63.18, -36.81), -25.70% (-31.68, -19.73), -26.80% (-33.63, -19.97) and -19.20% (-24.65, -13.75), respectively (all p-values &lt;0.0001).

For Lp(a), using the primary analysis method (ANCOVA), the LS mean between-group difference (95% CI) for change from baseline to Day 330 was -6.18% (-17.48, 5.12; p-value 0.1419). The supplementary analysis, using log transformation to account for the skewed distribution of this parameter, showed a ratio of geometric means inclisiran / placebo (95% CI) of 0.90 (0.80,1.00) in favour of inclisiran (p-value 0.0212).

Given the hierarchical nature of testing for the key secondary endpoints and the non-significant result for Lp(a) using the primary analysis method, time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C, absolute change in LDL-C, and percentage change in Apo B are considered statistically significant, whereas the other results are considered exploratory.
