Pre-specified sensitivity analyses for the key secondary endpoints used MMRM with control-based PMM for missing value imputation, except for the time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C, for which MMRM without multiple imputation (that assumes missing data as MAR) was utilised. Results of the sensitivity analyses were consistent with the primary analysis.

Subgroup results for the key secondary endpoints (time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C, absolute change in LDL-C, percent change in Apo B, non-HDL-C, total cholesterol) were consistent with subgroup results for the primary efficacy endpoint. More variability was seen for percent change in Lp(a).

As regards other secondary efficacy results, PCSK9 was reduced vs. baseline by -71.2% at Day 330 and by -76.3% in the inclisiran versus the placebo group. Reductions in LDL-C were observed by the first assessment at Day 90 and reached a maximum at Day 150 in the inclisiran group, which was mostly sustained over the remaining Part 1 treatment period (i.e., up to Day 360). In inclisiran-treated participants, clinically meaningful reductions in LDL-C were observed irrespective of their genetic variant group. Results for Apo B, Lp(a), non-HDL-C, and total cholesterol were also consistent across visits in Part 1. Modest improvements were observed in HDL-C and ApoA1, while no effect was seen on triglycerides and VLDL-C.

During the open-label Part 2 period, a consistent, clinically relevant reduction in LDL-C was observed with inclisiran, sustained over 2 years of treatment. The mean percent change in LDL-C (±SD) from baseline to Day 720 was -33.7% (±24.0) (-32.3% [±24.8] inclisiran-inclisiran group, -36.4% [±22.3] placebo-inclisiran group). Improvements were also seen in ApoB, non-HDL-C, and total cholesterol. PCSK9 levels were reduced from baseline at Day 720 by -71.6% (±13.4) (-71.4% [±13.4] inclisiraninclisiran, -72.2% [±13.5] placebo-inclisiran).

As regards key secondary efficacy endpoints, in Part 1 of the study, relevant reductions were seen for time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330; the betweengroup difference in mean percent change was -29.30% (-36.24, -22.36; p&lt;0.0001).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Relevant reductions were also seen for absolute change in LDL-C, and percent change in Apo B, nonHDL-C, and total cholesterol (all p-values &lt;0.0001), while results for Lp(a)were not statistically significant (p-value 0.1419) and , given the hierarchical testing of the key secondary endpoints, further results are to be considered exploratory.

Results of pre-specified sensitivity and subgroup analyses for the key secondary endpoints were consistent with the primary analysis.

During the open-label Part 2, consistent reductions were seen in LDL-C, sustained over 2 years of treatment. The mean percent change in LDL-C (±SD) from baseline to Day 720 was -33.7% (±24.0).

## Ancillary analyses

## Exploratory endpoints

As regards exploratory results, number and percentage of participants reaching individual response criteria of LDL-C thresholds or percent LDL-C reductions at Day 330 are summarised in the table below. The proportion of participants reaching the recommended LDL-C target for adolescents and children of &lt;130 mg/dL was 56.7% (51/90) in inclisiran versus 12.5% (6/48) in placebo treated participants; 27.8% (25/90) of participants in the inclisiran versus no participants in the placebo group reached a LDL-C of &lt;100 mg/dL. Lower LDL-C thresholds were only reached by few participants in the inclisiran group. A ≥50% reduction in LDL-C was reached by 16 participants in the inclisiran (17.8%, 16/90) and no participants in the placebo group.

The proportion of participants reaching the recommended LDL-C target for adolescents and children of &lt;130 mg/dL was 64.7% (90/139) overall, 62.6% (57/91) in the inclisiran-inclisiran, and 68.8% (33/48) in the placebo-inclisiran group; 38.1% (53/139) reached an LDL-C of &lt;100 mg/dL. Source: Table 14.2-4.1ii

LDL-C response by genotype subgroup analysis included only participants with adjudicated central laboratory genetic results (n=125/141). In the double-blind Part 1 period, consistent LDL-C lowering was seen with inclisiran across the included genetic variant groups; for the two main genotypes, LDLR null (negative) and LDLR defective, LDL-C reductions with inclisiran were -25.1% (n=45) and -31.0% (n=28), respectively. For the open-label Part 2 period, this analysis was not specified in the protocol as an exploratory endpoint but was added for completeness in the SAP. As for Part 1, consistent LDL-C lowering was seen with inclisiran across the included genetic variant groups. In the LDLR null (negative) group, LDL-C reductions were -32.7% overall (n=68; -30.0% inclisiran-inclisiran, -38.3% placebo-inclisiran) and in the LDLR defective group, reductions were -36.9% overall (n=47; -35.4% inclisiran-inclisiran, -38.9% placebo-inclisiran group).

The exploratory endpoint carotid intima media thickness (cIMT; right and left common carotid arteries) was measured at Day 1 (baseline), Day 330 (Year 1), and Day 720 (Year 2) by ultrasonography, using local machines. During the double-blind Part 1, mean cIMT (right, left, and average of right and left) at baseline and Day 330 were measured and changes from baseline to Day 330 in both treatment groups provided. No effect on cIMT was seen with inclisiran treatment following 1 year of treatment. The mean (±SD) baseline cIMT for the average of right and left cIMT was 0.482 ±0.081 mm in the inclisiran and 0.498 ±0.076 mm in the placebo group. At Day 330, the mean (±SD) changes from baseline were -0.003 ±0.056 mm in the inclisiran and -0.007 ±0.073 mm in placebo group. Changes from baseline to Day 720 (EoS; Part 2) in mean cIMT were -0.015 ±0.071 mm overall and -0.010 ±0.068 mm in the inclisiran-inclisiran and -0.026 ±0.078 mm in the placebo-inclisiran group.

## Pharmacokinetic results

An exploratory endpoint for this study was the evaluation of inclisiran concentrations. For pharmacokinetic results, see section Pharmacokinetics.

As regards exploratory results, 56.7% (51/90) in the inclisiran versus 12.5% (6/48) in placebo group reached an LDL-C &lt;130 mg/dL and 27.8% (25/90) versus none, respectively, an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. A ≥50% reduction in LDL-C was reached by 16/90 (17.8%) in the inclisiran and none in the placebo group. During the open-label Part 2, 90/139 (64.7%) participants reached an LDL-C &lt;130 mg/dL and 53/139 (38.1%) an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. A ≥20% reduction in LDL-C was reached by 105/139 (75.5%), ≥30% by 85/139 (61.2%), and ≥50% by 33/139 (23.7%) participants. Consistent LDL-C lowering was seen with inclisiran across the included genetic variant groups in Part 1. For the openlabel Part 2, such an analysis was not specified in the protocol but added for completeness in the SAP; as for Part 1, consistent LDL-C lowering was seen across the included genetic variant groups. No clinically relevant effect of inclisiran on carotid intima media thickness (cIMT) was seen during Part 1 or 2.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.4.3. Discussion on clinical efficacy

## Assessment of paediatric data on clinical efficacy

Inclisiran was first approved in the EU on 09 December 2020 under the proprietary name of Leqvio and is currently only licensed in adults, including patients with heterozygous, but not with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia, from 18 years of age onwards.

The completed paediatric studies ORION-13 (CKJX839C12302) and ORION-16 (CKJX839C12301) are part of agreed PIP for inclisiran and were intended to investigate the use of inclisiran in adolescents from ≥12 to &lt;18 years of age with homozygous (HoFH; ORION-13) or heterozygous (HeFH; ORION16) familial hypercholesterolaemia and elevated LDL-cholesterol.

Both were conducted as two-part, multicentre, randomised studies; Part 1 (year 1) investigated inclisiran versus placebo in a double-blind setting, while in Part 2 (year 2) all participants received open-label inclisiran.

## Homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HoFH) / Study ORION-13

Inclisiran is not licensed in adults with HoFH. In the phase III, two-part (double-blind placebocontrolled / open-label), multicentre study ORION-5 (MDCO-PCS-17-02) in adult patients with HoFH, inclisiran treatment did not reduce LDL-C levels despite substantial lowering of PCSK9 levels (Raal et al., 2024) 9 .

The paediatric study ORION-13 included 13 male and female adolescents, 9 in the inclisiran and 4 in the placebo group, with a genetic confirmation of HoFH, excluding LDLR null/null genotypes, and an LDL-C &gt;130 mg/dL who were on a maximally tolerated dose of an HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor (statin) with or without other lipid lowering therapy. One (1) participant in the placebo group was on LDL-apheresis.

For study ORION-13 no formal hypothesis testing was planned and none was performed.

All participants completed both parts of the study and were analysed.

In Part 1, the mean percent change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 was -21.6% in the inclisiran and +11.7% in the placebo group (mean between-group difference [95% CI] -33.25% [-59.17, -7.34]). However, a waterfall plot of individual percentage changes in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 by participant showed a variable magnitude; all participants in the inclisiran group showed a reduction in LDL-C albeit with significant differences in magnitude, while response in the placebo group was less consistent with an increase in LDL-C of 54.9% as well as a decrease of -15.4%. Two thirds (66.7%) of the participants in the inclisiran group achieved a &gt;15% and more than half (55.6%) a ≥20% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330. In line with the primary endpoint, the mean timeadjusted percent change in LDL-C (after Day 90 up to Day 330) was -21.0% in the inclisiran and 13.0% in the placebo group (between-group difference [95% CI] -33.97% [-67.54, -0.39]).

9 Raal F, Durst R, Bi R, Talloczy Z, Maheux P, Lesogor A, Kastelein JJP; ORION-5 Study Investigators. Efficacy, Safety, and Tolerability of Inclisiran in Patients With Homozygous Familial Hypercholesterolemia: Results From the ORION-5 Randomised Clinical Trial. Circulation. 2024 Jan 30;149(5):354-362. doi: 10.1161/CIRCULATIONAHA.122.063460. Epub 2023 Oct 18. PMID: 37850379; PMCID: PMC10815002.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The percentage changes from baseline in LDL-C in Part 1 were largely consistent across visits, but there were outliers. Reductions were seen for absolute change in LDL-C and percent change in ApoB, non-HDL-C, VLDL-C, and total cholesterol. No reduction was seen in Lp(a) and only small variable effects for triglycerides, HDL-C, and Apo A1. Supplementary analyses excluding the participant (n=1) on LDL-apheresis showed consistent results.

In Part 2, the extent of the overall mean change in LDL-C was lower compared to Part 1 (difference driven by 2 participants in inclisiran-inclisiran group with LDL-C of +27.3% and +47.4%, respectively). A waterfall plot of percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720 showed a variable magnitude; 10/13 participants had a decrease in LDL-C at Day 720, while in 3/13 participants, LDL-C increased at Day 720.

As regards exploratory results, in Part 1, only 1 participant in the inclisiran group reached the prespecified LDL-C threshold of &lt;130 mg/dL and none an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. In Part 2, 2 participants reached the pre-specified LDL-C threshold of &lt;130 mg/dL, 1 participant an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL, and no participant an LDL-C below 70 mg/dL. Consistent LDL-C lowering was seen across the included genetic variants. No clinically relevant effect of inclisiran on carotid intima media thickness (cIMT) was seen. Since only 1 participant was treated with LDL-apheresis, no relevant analysis is feasible.

While the observed reductions in LDL-C and other lipid parameters in study ORION-13 are acknowledged, the very low number of participants and the exploratory design of the study (including the absence of formal hypothesis testing) preclude robust conclusions regarding efficacy in this population. Furthermore, inclisiran is currently not approved in adults with HoFH, as study ORION-5 in adult patients with HoFH failed to demonstrate a relevant effect of inclisiran in this patient population, thereby limiting transferability of the data.

Within current application the MAH submitted a variation application under category C.I.6 of the variation classification Guideline with the scope to include C.I.6: \"Extension of indication to include the treatment of paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HoFH) for LEQVIO based on the final results from study CKJX839C12302 (ORION-13). ORION-13 is a two part (double-blind inclisiran versus placebo [Year 1] followed by openlabel inclisiran [Year 2]) randomised multicentre study to evaluate safety, tolerability, and efficacy of inclisiran in paediatric patients (12 to less than 18 years) with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia and elevated LDL-cholesterol. \"

Based on the assessment of the data contained in the application, the CHMP is of the view that the following changes to the MA should be introduced \"Update to sections 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC to include the treatment of paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HoFH) based on the final results from study CKJX839C12302 (ORION-13). ORION-13 is a two part (double-blind inclisiran versus placebo [Year 1] followed by open-label inclisiran [Year 2]) randomised multicentre study to evaluate safety, tolerability, and efficacy of inclisiran in paediatric patients (12 to less than 18 years) with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia and elevated LDL-cholesterol.\" These changes fall under category C.4 of the variation classification Guideline.

During the procedure, the MAH announced that it no longer wishes to pursue the proposed indication in paediatric patients aged 12 to &lt;18 years with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HoFH). In reply to the Request for Supplementary Information, the MAH updated the electronic Application Form and the product information accordingly.

The CHMP considered that the results of paediatric study ORION-13 are adequately reflected in sections 4.8, 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HeFH) / Study ORION-16

Study ORION-16 in adolescents with HeFH included 141 participants and 139 of them completed Part 1 and Part 2. The majority of participants had a genetic diagnosis of HeFH.

The study met the primary endpoint of superiority of inclisiran compared to placebo in reducing LDL-C in adolescents with HeFH on maximally tolerated statin treatment. The between-group difference (95% CI) of -28.54% (-35.81, -21.27; p&lt;0.0001) between inclisiran and placebo is considered clinically relevant. Furthermore, in Part 1 relevant reductions were seen for key secondary efficacy endpoints (time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 up to Day 330 between-group difference mean percent change -29.30% [-36.24, -22.36; p&lt;0.0001]; absolute change in LDL-C, percent change in Apo B, non-HDL-C, total cholesterol all p&lt;0.0001). Results for Lp(a)were not statistically significant. Results of pre-specified sensitivity and subgroup analyses were consistent with the primary analysis and during Part 2, consistent reductions in LDL-C were seen.

For the exploratory endpoints concerning individual responsiveness of participants, relevant effects of inclisiran versus placebo were seen in the percentage of participants reaching an LDL-C &lt;130 mg/dL and &lt;100 mg/dL as well as a ≥50% reduction in LDL-C. Consistent LDL-C lowering was seen with inclisiran across the included genetic variant groups in Part 1. No clinically relevant effect of inclisiran on cIMT was seen.

Overall, it can be agreed that clinically meaningful effects were shown in HeFH participants in Study Orion-16. However, interpretation of the provided data is limited by the low number of participants included in study Orion 16 (141 participants, 93 inclisiran, 48 placebo) as well as by the open-label uncontrolled design of Part 2.

Furthermore, the applicantwas asked to reconsider dose finding in adolescents. The applicant did not provide a different body weight limit for the dose of 300mg in the paediatric population but proposed to retain the proposed dosing strategy focussing on the benign safety profile of inclisiran. The applicant argued that kidney and liver function were considered mature by 2 years of age and above that age were expected to be equivalent to adults, citing a publication by Boven 2013. Overall, due to the good safety profile of inclisiran, the proposed and studied dosing regimen is considered acceptable.

Thus, it is noted that subjects were on maximally tolerated dose of statins with or without other lipidlowering therapy. The Applicant was asked to clarify if any of the subjects reduced the dose of concurrently taken statins or other lipid-lowering agents during the Study. The applicant clarified that during Part 1 of study ORION-16, where type and dose of lipid-lowering therapy was not to be altered, none of the participants in either group reduced the dose of concomitant background statin or other lipid-lowering therapy, while during the open-label Part 2, dose reductions in concomitant background statin / lipid-lowering medications were reported for 3/139 (2.2%) participants. It was agreed that these changes are not considered to have significantly influenced efficacy of inclisiran in trial ORION16.

Furthermore, the overall number of protocol deviations in the Study was considered quite high - overall, 12.1% (17/141) of participants reported at least one protocol deviation, 16.1% (15/93) in the inclisiran group and 4.2% (2/48) in the placebo group. The Applicant was asked to comment on possible reasons for the reported disproportion of protocol deviations between the inclisiran and placebo groups during Part 1 of the Study (OC). The applicant responded that protocol deviations occurred only in the category \"other\" (12.9% [12/93] inclisiran, 2.1% [1/48] placebo); only 1 of these deviations in the inclisiran group occurred during the treatment period, the other 11 deviations occurred before participants received the first dose of inclisiran, either related to the separate consents required for the optional pharmacogenomics or PK assessments, or related to Interactive Response Technology (IRT) procedures, including randomisation calls before the actual randomisation visit. It was concluded that the observed imbalance in protocol deviations between both groups are not considered to have possibly affected conclusions on efficacy or safety in study ORION-16.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In addition, it is noted that after the Part 2/Year 2 database lock, an implausible increase in Lp(a) at Day 720 was observed for one participant (inclisiran-inclisiran group) and that another participant (inclisiran-inclisiran group), who had the Day 720 visit at the same investigational site on the same day, showed an atypical decrease in Lp(a) at Day 720. The Applicant assumed that the possible reason was a sample mix-up between both participants and has implemented precautionary measures to prevent such cases in two studies with younger children.

## 2.4.4. Conclusions on the clinical efficacy

As regards the use of inclisiran in adolescents ≥12 years of age with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (Study ORION-16), the efficacy data provided from this study together with the available efficacy data from the use of inclisiran in the adult population with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia, are, in general, considered to support the proposed relevant change to the indication (in paediatric patients aged 12 years and older with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HeFH): in combination with a statin or statin with other lipid-lowering therapies in patients unable to reach LDL-C goals with the maximum tolerated dose of a statin, or alone or in combination with other lipid-lowering therapies in patients who are statin-intolerant, or for whom a statin is contraindicated).

As regards the use of inclisiran in paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HoFH) in combination with other lipid-lowering therapies (Study ORION-13), the available efficacy data were considered insufficient to support the proposed indication. During the procedure, the applicant announced that it no longer wishes to pursue the proposed HoFH indication. However, a concise summary of study ORION-13 was reflected in sections 4.8, 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC with corresponding change of scope from C.I.6 to variation category C.4 of the Variation Classification Guideline.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.5. Clinical safety

## Introduction

The safety of inclisiran in adults has been characterised and assessed from the Phase III development program, consisting of three pivotal Phase III studies (ORION-9 / ORION-10 / ORION-11) with 18months duration, including data from 1833 adults with ASCVD, ASCVD risk equivalents, and/or HeFH exposed to inclisiran on top of maximally tolerated statin therapy, and from two extension studies (ORION-3, extension to Phase II study ORION-1; ORION-8, extension to Phase III studies ORION-9 / - 10 / -11 and to ORION-3).

Data from these studies indicate a favourable safety profile in adults. A higher incidence of injection site reactions is an identified common adverse drug reaction (ADR) of inclisiran (8.2% inclisiran, 1.8% placebo). These injection site ADRs were mild or moderate in severity, transient, and resolved without sequelae. The most frequently ADRs at the injection site were injection site reaction (3.1%), injection site pain (2.2%), injection site erythema (1.6%), and injection site rash (0.7%). There were more frequent elevations of serum hepatic transaminases between &gt;1x the upper limit of normal (ULN) and ≤3x ULN in patients on inclisiran (ALT: 19.7%, AST: 17.2%) than on placebo (ALT: 13.6%, AST: 11.1%); these elevations did not progress to &gt;3x ULN, were asymptomatic, and not associated with adverse reactions or other evidence of liver dysfunction. In patients tested for anti-drug antibodies in the pivotal trials, confirmed positivity was detected in 1.8% prior to dosing and 4.9% during the 18 months of treatment with inclisiran; no clinically significant differences in the clinical efficacy, safety, or pharmacodynamic profiles of inclisiran were observed in the patients who tested positive for antiinclisiran antibodies. No overall differences in safety were observed between these elderly and younger patients. Inclisiran is not associated with an increased risk for renal dysfunction, hypersensitivity, or neurologic events / neurocognitive disorders, and there was no difference from placebo in new onset or worsening of diabetes.

So far, more than 30,000 patients have been exposed to inclisiran in completed and ongoing clinical trials and the exposure to inclisiran from post-marketing experience was 368,582 patient treatment years as of 31 December 2024 (PSUR 01 January 2024 to 31 December 2024).

For the current application, the existing safety data in adults have been complemented by safety data from the use of inclisiran in the treatment of adolescents from 12 to less than 18 years of age with heterozygous (HeFH) and with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HoFH). These safety data are from the two pivotal adolescent clinical studies CKJX839C12302 (ORION-13) for HoFH and CKJX839C12301 (ORION-16) for HeFH. Furthermore, safety data from the clinical study CKJX839A12302 (ORION-5) in adults with HoFH have been included in the submission as supportive data. Also, supportive safety data from the ongoing extension study CKJX839C12001B (VICTORIONPEDS-OLE) in participants who have completed ORION-13 or ORION-16 have been provided.

Safety results for both pivotal studies in this application have been presented separately by the applicant, arguing that HoFH and HeFH are different disease manifestations in severity, prevalence, and treatment modalities and that the sample size of study ORION-13 (HoFH) was very small (n = 13).

## Patient exposure

Studies ORION-13 (HoFH) and ORION-16 (HeFH) both had a 1 year double-blind inclisiran versus placebo (2:1) Part 1, followed by a 1 year open-label inclisiran Part 2. Participants were on a maximally tolerated dose of statin at the investigator's discretion, and study ORION-13 (HoFH) included 13 and ORION-16 (HeFH) 141 participants. For demographic and baseline characteristics of studies ORION-13 and ORION-16 please see Clinical efficacy section, above.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The design of study ORION-5 (HoFH) used for supportive safety information, differed compared to studies ORION-13 and ORION-16 as Part 1 covered only 6 months double-blind inclisiran versus placebo, followed by an 18 months open-label inclisiran Part 2. Study ORION-5 included 56 adult participants ≥18 years of age, with a genetic and/or clinical diagnosis of HoFH and fasting LDL-C ≥130 mg/dL (3.4 mmol/L). Demographic and baseline characteristics were overall comparable between the treatment groups; 60.7% were female, the median age was 41.0 years (range 22-70 years), the mean BMI 26.2 kg/m 2 , the median baseline eGFR 95.5 mL/min/1.73 m 2 , and 85.7% of participants were white. All participants used high intensity statins, 33.9% statins only, 66.1% other lipid-lowering therapy (mostly ezetimibe), and 35.7% were on LDL-apheresis. Overall, 67.9% had a history of ASCVD, all of whom had prior or current CHD, 5.4% had diabetes mellitus, 37.5% had hypertension, and 89.3% had a prior or current history of tendon xanthomas. Participants were categorised in the genotype groups homozygous LDLR, compound heterozygous LDLR, other genetic types, and double null alleles for LDLR; 13/56 (23.2%) had double null alleles for LDLR (LDLR null/null genotype), 10/37 (27.0%) in the inclisiran and 3/19 (15.8%) in the placebo group. All participants had at least 1 concomitant medication at Day 1 of the study; the most common drug classes in addition to LMTs were antithrombotic agents (71.4%), beta blocking agents (57.1%), and plain ACE inhibitors (28.6%).

VICTORION-PEDS-OLE is an ongoing open-label, single arm, multicentre extension study in participants with HoFH or HeFH who have completed studies ORION-13 or ORION-16 and derived a benefit in the investigator's judgement. The purpose of the study is to provide continued access to treatment with inclisiran and to evaluate longer-term safety and tolerability. Selected safety data (deaths, SAEs, AEs leading to study drug discontinuation, pregnancy) with a data cut-off date of 02 January 2025 have been provided as supportive. Approximately half of the enrolled participants are female (55.2%, 69/125). The mean age of participants was 17.1 years at time of entry into the study (40.8% ≥18 years of age).

## ORION-13 HoFH

In the double-blind Part 1, the mean duration on study was identical to the mean duration of exposure to study treatment in both treatment groups and they were comparable between groups (360.6 days inclisiran, 364.0 days placebo).

During the open-label Part 2, the mean duration on study and the mean duration of exposure to study treatment were both 358.5 days overall, with comparable durations in the inclisiran-inclisiran and placebo-inclisiran groups.

Over the entire study duration, all participants were administered all 6 scheduled study doses of study drug. Overall, the mean duration on study and the mean duration of exposure to study treatment were both 719.1 days with comparable durations in the inclisiran-inclisiran and the placebo-inclisiran group. Patient years of exposure to study treatment were 25.6 overall, 17.7 in the inclisiran-inclisiran, and 7.9 in the placebo-inclisiran group.

## ORION-16 HeFH

In the double-blind Part 1 study period, the mean duration on study was identical to the mean duration of exposure to study treatment in both treatment groups and they were comparable between both groups (357.9 days inclisiran, 362.9 days placebo).

During the open-label Part 2, the mean duration on study was 361.1 days and the mean duration of exposure to study treatment 360.6 days overall, with comparable durations in the inclisiran-inclisiran and placebo-inclisiran groups.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Over the entire study duration, 97.9% (138/141) of participants were administered all 6 scheduled doses of study drug, 96.8% (90/93) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 100% (48/48) in the placeboinclisiran group. The mean duration on study was 714.6 days and the mean duration of exposure to study treatment 714.1 days overall, with comparable durations in the inclisiran-inclisiran and placeboinclisiran groups. The overall patient years of exposure to study treatment were 275.6, 180.5 in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 95.1 in the placebo-inclisiran group.

## Supportive safety data

## ORION-5 (adult HoFH)

In the 6 month double-blind Part 1, 94.6% of participants in the inclisiran and 100% in the placebo group received both scheduled doses (Day 1, Day 90). The subject year exposure to inclisiran was 18.6.

In the open-label Part 2, 82.4% in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 89.5% in the placebo-inclisiran group received all scheduled doses of study medication. The subject year exposure to inclisiran was 65.5 in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 29.2 in the placebo-inclisiran group.

Overall, 94.6% (53/56) completed Part 1; reasons for discontinuation prior to Day 180 were withdrawal of consent (2 participants) and other (1 participant), all in the inclisiran group. Of the 53 participants entering Part 2, 47 (88.7%) completed Part 2; reasons for discontinuation included death (3 participants) and withdrawal of consent (2 participants) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and \"other\" (1 participant) in the placebo-inclisiran group.

## VICTORION-PEDS-OLE

From studies ORION-13 and ORION-16, 9/13 and 116/139 participants, respectively, were enrolled. At the time of the data cut-off, no participants had completed the study and 23.2% (29/125; 33.3% [3/9] ORION-13, 22.4% [26/116] ORION-16) discontinued from the study. The primary reason for discontinuation was post-study access to treatment (19.2%, 24/125), which occurred in line with the corresponding protocol stopping criteria (applicable after 1 year) for participants reaching the local legal age and inclisiran being commercially available in the adult population. In addition, 5 participants discontinued from the study due to a subject or guardian decision. Study treatment disposition was identical to study disposition, except that 7 additional participants were marked as having discontinued study treatment due to post-study access to treatment; since the early exit visits for these participants had not taken place at the time of the data cut-off date, this was not yet reflected in the study discontinuation count.

The mean duration on study and mean duration of exposure was 230.6 ±174.0 days and 230.4 ±173.8 days, respectively. The patient years of exposure to inclisiran in VICTORION-PEDS-OLE was 78.9, and the cumulative patient-year exposure to inclisiran over the feeder studies and VICTORIONPEDS-OLE was 284.3.

## Adverse events

## ORION-13 HoFH

During Part 1, 7/9 (77.8%) inclisiran- and 1/4 (25.0%) placebo-treated participants experienced at least 1 treatment-emergent adverse events (AEs). Two (2/9; 22.2%) participants in the inclisiran and none in the placebo group experienced a study drug related treatment-emergent AE; the difference was exclusively driven by mild injection site reactions (see below).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

No treatment-emergent SAEs and no discontinuation of study drug due to treatment-emergent AEs were reported in either group.

There were no deaths in Part 1.

## Common adverse events

Common AEs (≥15% total group) by PT are presented in the table below. In Part 1, the most common AE was COVID-19, reported in 4/9 (44.4%) participants in the inclisiran and 1/4 (25.0%) in the placebo group. The AEs of COVID-19 in the inclisiran group were mild in severity, considered not related to study drug, and resolved within 6 to 9 days. For 1 participant (007), the AE of COVID-19 resolved after Day 360 and was noted in the electronic data capture system after the data cut-off date. The AE of COVID-19 in the placebo group was mild in severity, considered not related to study drug, and resolved within 10 days. Other common AEs were abdominal pain upper, gastroenteritis, pyrexia, and injection site reaction, which were reported by ≤2 participants in either treatment group. Gastroenteritis was reported by 1/9 (11.1%) in the inclisiran and 1/4 (25.0%) in the placebo group. Abdominal pain upper and pyrexia were experienced by 2/9 (22.2%) participants in the inclisiran and no participants in placebo group. The AEs of abdominal pain upper, gastroenteritis, and pyrexia in the inclisiran group were all mild in severity, considered not related to study drug, and resolved within a few days. The AE of gastroenteritis in the placebo group was moderate in severity, considered not related to study drug, and resolved within 6 days. Injection site reactions (all mild) occurred in 2/9 (22.2%) participants in the inclisiran and no participants in the placebo group (see below).

## Severity of adverse events

In Part 1, the majority of reported AEs were mild; 6/9 (66.7%) inclisiran-treated and 0/4 (0%) placebo-treated participants reported AEs with a maximum severity of mild. Moderate AEs were seen in 1/9 (11.1%) participants (PT of conjunctivitis) in the inclisiran and 1/4 (25.0%; PT of gastroenteritis) in the placebo group. No participant experienced severe events.

During Part 2 (Open-label period), all reported AEs were either mild or moderate, with 4/13 (30.8%) participants each reporting AEs with a maximum severity of mild and moderate, respectively. AEs with a maximum severity of mild were reported by 4/9 (44.4%) participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran and no participant in the placebo-inclisiran group. AEs with a maximum severity of moderate were reported in 1/9 (11.1%) participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 3/4 (75.0%) participants in the placeboinclisiran group. No participants experienced severe events.

## ORION-16 HeFH

During Part 1, 76.3% (71/93) of the participants in the inclisiran group experienced at least 1 treatment-emergent AE compared to 66.7% (32/48) in the placebo group (see table below). Also, more participants in the inclisiran (17.2%, 16/93) than in the placebo group (8.3%, 4/48) experienced a study drug related treatment-emergent AE, the difference being driven by a higher incidence of mild injection site reactions with inclisiran. No other clinically important differences in AEs between treatment groups were seen.

The incidence of treatment-emergent SAEs was low and comparable between the treatment groups, 3.2% (3/93) in the inclisiran and 2.1% (1/48) in the placebo group; none were considered related to study drug.

One (1) participant in the inclisiran group discontinued study drug due to mild treatment-emergent AEs of nausea and vertigo.

There were no deaths in Part 1.

In Part 2, 62.6% (87/139) of participants overall experienced at least 1 treatment-emergent AE (65.9% [60/91] inclisiran-inclisiran, 56.3% [27/48] placebo-inclisiran) and 10.1% (14/139) a study drug related treatment-emergent AE (13.2% [12/91] inclisiran-inclisiran, 4.2% [2/48] placeboinclisiran). Thirteen (13) of the 14 participants reporting study drug-related AEs had mild injection site reactions.

The incidence of treatment-emergent SAEs was low (4.3% [6/139] overall, 4.4% [4/91] inclisiraninclisiran, 4.2% [2/48] placebo-inclisiran) and none were considered related to study drug.

There were no discontinuations of study drug due to treatment-emergent AEs and no deaths in the open-label Part 2.

## Common adverse events

Common AEs (≥3% total group) by PT for Part 1 are presented in the table below. The most frequently observed AE was COVID-19 (16.1% [15/93] inclisiran vs. 25.0% [12/48] placebo). Other most common AEs were nasopharyngitis (14.0% [13/93] vs. 18.8% [9/48]), influenza (10.8% [10/93] vs. 12.5% [6/48]), and headache (12.9% [12/93] vs. 6.3% [3/48]). The majority (66.7%, 8/12) of participants reporting headaches in the inclisiran group had mild AEs, none were severe or serious; in most participants (83.3%, 10/12) the headaches were considered not related to study drug and there was no time correlation to inclisiran administration. In addition, many participants (75%, 9/12) reporting a headache in the inclisiran group had a relevant medical history of headache (2), psychiatric disorders (attention deficit hyperactivity disorder, anxiety/depression) (3), or obesity (6); in 1 case the headache was judged to be a side effect of beta blocker use. All 5 events of syncope in the inclisiran group were considered not related to study drug, none was severe, serious, or had a recurrence and there was no time correlation to inclisiran administration. In 3 of the 5 cases clear triggers could be identified, as reported by the investigators, which were being in a hot and overcrowded [Circumstances] environment, emotional stress, and change from lying down to standing, respectively.

The AEs in the Investigations SOC in the inclisiran group were \"SARS-COVID-2 test positive\" reported in 2 participants and different PTs of mild laboratory abnormalities, all considered not related to study drug.

## Severity of adverse events

In Part 1, the majority of participants had AEs, which were mild as maximum severity, 60.6% (43/71) in the inclisiran and 62.5% (20/32) in the placebo group. Severe AEs were only reported in 2 participants in the inclisiran group (SAEs of anaphylactic reaction to [Circumstances] and Status migrainosus, both not considered related to study drug).

In Part 2, all participants except 1 reported AEs that were mild or moderate, with 33.8% (47/139) of participants experiencing AEs with a maximum severity of mild and 28.1% (39/139) with a maximum severity of moderate. A maximum severity of mild was reported by 36.3% (33/91) in the inclisiraninclisiran and 29.2% (14/48) in the placebo-inclisiran group and a maximum severity of moderate in 29.7% (27/91) and 25.0% (12/48), respectively. Only 1 participant (0.7%, 1/139) in the placeboinclisiran group experienced a severe AE, which was post-traumatic stress disorder after the participant [Circumstances].; the participant was treated with lorazepam and zoplicone for 1 month and fully recovered.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In Part 1, the incidence of AEs considered related to study drug was 17.2% (16/93) in the inclisiran and 8.3% (4/48) in the placebo group. The difference in study drug-related AEs was driven by AEs of mild injection site reactions. Fifteen (15/16) participants in the inclisiran group reported study drugrelated AEs of injection site reaction. Only a few participants reported other study-drug related AEs, which were balanced between treatment groups.

Only 1 participant reported another study-drug-related AE (mild abdominal pain).

## Supportive safety data

ORION-5 (adult HoFH)

In the 6 months double-blind Part 1, the incidences of treatment-emergent AEs were 35.1% in the inclisiran and 31.6% in the placebo group; incidences of SAEs were 5.4% and 5.3%, respectively. No treatment-emergent SAEs were considered related to study drug and there were no discontinuations of study drug due to treatment-emergent AEs. No participants died during Part 1 of the study.

In the 18 months open-label, uncontrolled Part 2, 56.6% had at least 1 treatment-emergent AE, 55.9% in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 57.9% in the placebo-inclisiran group. The incidence of treatment-emergent SAEs was 20.8%, 23.5% in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 15.8% in the placeboinclisiran group; these were not considered related to study drug. There were no discontinuations of study drug due to treatment-emergent AEs. Three (3) participants died during Part 2.

## Serious adverse event / deaths / other significant events

## ORION-13 HoFH

Deaths and serious adverse events

No participants died and no SAEs were reported during either part of the study.

## Injection site reactions

During Part 1, 2/9 (22.2%) participants on inclisiran and none (0/4) on placebo reported AEs of injection site reactions. The reported PTs were injection site reaction (2 participants) and injection site erythema (1 participant). All injection site reactions were mild and none were serious or led to study drug discontinuation; all were transient and resolved without sequelae.

In Part 2, 1/13 (7.7%) participant reported an injection site reaction. This was a single AE in a participant in the inclisiran-inclisiran group, with a reported PT of injection site erythema. This injection site reaction was mild, not serious, transient, resolved without sequelae, and did not lead to study drug discontinuation.

## Other safety topics

In Part 1, no cardiac, hepatic, musculoskeletal, neurological, psychiatric, or renal events in either treatment group were reported and no participant experienced AEs of new onset or worsening of diabetes. There were also no ECG-related AEs. One (1/9, 11.1%) participant in the inclisiran and 1/4 (25.0%) in the placebo group reported non-serious events of hypersensitivity that were considered not related to study drug.

Also, no cardiac, hepatic, hypersensitivity, neurological, psychiatric, renal, or ECG-related AEs were reported for Part 2 and no participants experienced AEs of new onset or worsening of diabetes. Two

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

(2/13, 15.4%) participants, 1 in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 1 in the placebo-inclisiran group, reported a musculoskeletal event (costochondritis and ligament sprain, respectively) that were both considered not related to study drug.

## ORION-16 HeFH

## Deaths and serious adverse events

No participants died during either part of the study.

The incidence of SAEs during Part 1 was low (2.8% [4/141]) and comparable between treatment groups (3.2% [3/93] inclisiran, 2.1% [1/48] placebo). The 4 SAEs were in different SOCs, all were considered not related to study treatment, and all were reported as resolved. The SAEs in the inclisiran group were anaphylactic reaction to lactose [Circumstances] in a participant with a medical history of [Medical History], Dengue fever, and hospitalisation to initiate dihydroergotamine for worsening headaches (status migrainosus) after failed outpatient therapy in a participant with a history of [Medical History] and [Medical History]. The SAE in the placebo group was an episode of supraventricular tachycardia in a participant with a medical history of intermittent [Medical History].

The incidence of SAEs was also low in Part 2 (4.3% [6/139] overall, 4.4% [4/91] inclisiran-inclisiran, 4.2% [2/48] placebo-inclisiran). The 6 SAEs were in different SOCs, all were considered not related to study treatment, and all were reported as resolved. The SAEs in the inclisiran-inclisiran group were anaphylactic reaction to [Circumstances] in a participant with a medical history of [Medical History], [Medical History] in a participant with a medical history of [Medical History], blood CK increased, asymptomatic and considered by the investigator as caused by rosuvastatin, and varicose veins that led to hospitalisation for a scheduled stripping. The SAEs in the inclisiran-placebo group were concussion, due to an accident, and syncope secondary to a painful stimulus.

## Injection site reactions

In Part 1, a higher incidence of AEs related to injection site reactions was observed in the inclisiran compared to the placebo group (16.1% [15/93] and 6.3% [3/48], respectively). All injection site reactions were mild, none were serious or led to study drug discontinuation, and all were transient and resolved without sequelae.

During Part 2, a total of 9.4% (13/139) of participants reported at least one injection site reaction (12.1% [(11/91] inclisiran-inclisiran, 4.2% [2/48] placebo inclisiran). All reactions were mild, none were serious or led to study drug discontinuation, and all were transient and resolved without sequelae. Most participants (10/13) reporting an injection site reaction in Part 2 had already experienced an injection site reaction in Part 1 (8/11 inclisiran-inclisiran, 2/2 placebo-inclisiran). The majority of participants (10/13; all inclisiran-inclisiran), had an isolated injection site reaction in this study part.

## Other safety topics

During Part 1, there were no cardiac, neurological, renal events, or ECG-related AEs in either treatment group and no participants experienced AEs of new onset or worsening of diabetes. Musculoskeletal events (14.0% [13/93] inclisiran, 12.5% [6/48] placebo) and hypersensitivity events (4.3% [4/93] inclisiran, 6.3% [3/48] placebo) were balanced between groups. Hepatic and psychiatric events occurred at a low frequency in the inclisiran group (3.2% [3/93] and 4.3% [4/93], respectively), while no such events (0% [0/48]) occurred in the placebo group; these events were not considered related to study drug. The hepatic AEs were mild, transient elevations in liver function tests, including 1 case of mild hepatic steatosis in a participant with medical history of [Medical History] and [Medical History]. The psychiatric events were anxiety, insomnia, obstructive sleep apnoea syndrome, and tension headache (none were serious or severe) in participants with corresponding relevant medical histories.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In Part 2, there were also no cardiac, neurological, or renal events. Hepatic, hypersensitivity, musculoskeletal, and psychiatric events occurred at a similar frequency as during Part 1 and none of these events were considered related to study drug. The 2 hepatic AEs (1.4%) were mild, transient elevations in LFTs. The hypersensitivity events included an anaphylactic reaction to [Circumstances]. One (1) participant with a family history of [Medical History] was diagnosed with type 1 diabetes, considered not related to study drug; no participants experienced AEs of new onset or worsening of type 2 diabetes.

## Supportive safety data

## ORION-5 (adult HoFH)

## Deaths and serious adverse events

In Part 1, no participants died. In Part 2, 3 (8.8%) deaths occurred in the inclisiran-inclisiran group; these were not considered related to the study drug by the investigators. The causes of death by PT were multiple organ dysfunction syndrome, sudden cardiac death, and pneumonia viral in participants with coronary heart disease.

The overall incidence of SAEs in Part 1 was low and comparable between treatment groups (5.4% inclisiran [non-cardiac chest pain, endocarditis, adenocarcinoma of the colon]; 5.3% placebo [family stress]). None of the SAEs were considered related to study treatment. In Part 2, SAEs were reported in 20.8% (23.5% inclisiran-inclisiran, 15.8% placebo-inclisiran). The SOC categories with the highest proportion of participants were Infections and infestations (9.4%) and Cardiac disorders (5.7%). The most common SAEs by PT were aortic valve stenosis and pneumonia viral (each 3.8%, 2/53). None of the SAEs were considered related to study treatment.

## Other safety topics

In Part 1, there was no incidence of MACE, neurological events / neurocognitive disorders, renal events, or AEs related to new onset or worsening of diabetes. No participant in the inclisiran and 1 (5.3%) in the placebo group experienced a hepatic-related event (PTs of ALT and AST increased). The incidence of hypersensitivity events was 2.7% in the inclisiran (PT mouth ulceration) and 5.3% in the placebo group (PT of rhinitis allergic). There were no potentially clinically significant changes in biomarkers for diabetes, myopathy, or renal function.

In Part 2, 2 (5.9%) participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran-group experienced a MACE (PTs sudden death and angina unstable). Two (2; 5.9%) participants had a hepatic AE of INR increased and 1 participant (2.9%) had an AE of LFT abnormal in the inclisiran-inclisiran group and in the placeboinclisiran group, 1 (5.3%) participant had hepatic events of INR increased, ALT increased, and AST increased and 1 (5.3%) had an AE of ALT abnormal; all of the hepatic events were mild to moderate in severity and were not considered related to study drug. Two (2; 5.9%) participants in the inclisiraninclisiran group had renal-related events (acute kidney injury and mild renal impairment); both were not considered related to study treatment and had the outcome recovered / resolved. Four (4; 11.8%) participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 1 (5.3%) in the placebo-inclisiran group had TEAEs related to a new onset or worsening of diabetes; these events were either mild or moderate in severity and were not considered related to study drug. One (1; 2.9%) participant each in the inclisiran-inclisiran group had AEs related to hypersensitivity (PT rhinitis allergic) and neurological events / neurocognitive disorders (PTs neuralgia and neuropathy peripheral), all not considered related to study drug.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## VICTORION-PEDS-OLE

Deaths and serious adverse events

No participants died.

The incidence of treatment-emergent SAEs was 3.2% (4/125); all 4 participants derived from study ORION-16 in HeFH. These SAEs were in different SOCs and all were not considered related to study drug. The SAEs were gastroenteritis of unknown cause in a participant with a medical history of [Medical History], the AE resolved within 3 days following treatment with an antibiotic and a proton pump inhibitor; mastoid disorder, i.e. leakage from a previous [Medical History] in a participant with a medical history of [Medical History], which was described by the investigator as a common complication of the procedure, hospitalisation for worsening of phimosis and surgical procedure to resolve the condition in a participant with medical history of [Medical History]; [Medical History], i.e. two episodes of worsening cervicolumbar pain in a participant with a medical history of intermittent [Medical History]. The SAEs were reported as resolved or resolved with sequelae (spinal pain) at the time of the data cut-off date. See also tables below.

## Laboratory findings

## ORION-13 HoFH

Clinical laboratory evaluations, physical findings, and other observations

During Part 1, there were no clinically meaningful changes or between-group differences in safety laboratory parameters of haematology or biochemistry. No adverse effects regarding growth (height, weight, BMI), pubertal development (Tanner staging), or hormonal status (oestradiol in females, testosterone in males, LH, FSH, DHEA-S, cortisol) were seen with inclisiran treatment. There were no clinically meaningful differences in sex hormones between the inclisiran and placebo groups and the results for sex hormones and Tanner stage in males and females were consistent with the expected progression through puberty in the age groups that were studied. Height and body weight increased over time in both groups. The mean (±SD) height increases from baseline to Day 330 in the inclisiran and placebo groups were 2.2 cm (±3.02) and 2.3 cm (±1.25), respectively; the mean baseline height was lower in the inclisiran (156.6 cm) than the placebo group (169.9 cm). Consistent increases were also seen for body weight and mean changes in BMI were minimal in both treatment groups.

In Part 2, there were also no clinically meaningful changes in safety laboratory parameters of haematology or biochemistry and no adverse effects regarding growth (height, weight, BMI), pubertal development (Tanner staging), or hormonal status (oestradiol females, testosterone males, LH, FSH, DHEA-S, cortisol) were seen with inclisiran treatment. No notable changes from baseline in sex hormones were seen and the results for sex hormones and Tanner stages were consistent with the expected progression through puberty in the age groups studied. Regarding the Tanner assessment at EoS (Day 720), in males, for genitalia and pubic hair stage, the number of participants who either had a change in Tanner stage or were already at the maximum Tanner stage at baseline was 2/2 (100%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 1/2 (50%) in the placebo-inclisiran group. In females, for breast development stage, the number of participants who either had a change in Tanner stage or were already at the maximum Tanner stage at baseline was 6/7 (85.7%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 2/2 (100%) in the placebo-inclisiran group; for pubic hair stage, this were 5/7 (71.4%) and 1/2 (50.0%), respectively. Height and body weight increased over time during the 2-year study duration. The mean (±SD) height increase at EoS (Day 720) was 4.9 cm (±5.93) in the inclisiran-inclisiran (mean baseline 156.6 cm) and 3.8 cm (±3.59) in the placebo-inclisiran group (mean baseline 169.9 cm). Consistent increases were also seen for body weight; mean changes in BMI were minimal.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Immunogenicity (ADA measurements)

For the analysis of formation of ADA to inclisiran, whole blood study samples were collected in part 1 on Day 1 prior to study drug (inclisiran or placebo) injection (referred to as Day 1 0h) and 4 hours after injection (referred to as Day 1 +4h), as well as on Day 150 and Day 330.

In Part 2 (Year 2), samples for the ADA analysis collected on Day 510 and Day 720. In both parts, if necessary, unscheduled or Early Termination (ET) study samples were also to be collected and analysed.

Whole blood study samples from the ORION-13 Clinical Study No. CKJX839C12302 were processed into serum and recovered serum study samples were shipped to the Test Site on dry ice for analysis.

## Part 1 (Double-blind period)

The frequency of participants experiencing treatment-induced persistent and transient ADA is presented in the table below. Persistent and transient ADA were assessed using the definitions proposed by Shankar et al (2014). There were no persistent or transient treatment-induced ADA responses reported in either the inclisiran or the placebo treatment group. One participant in the inclisiran group had a positive ADA result at the pre-dose timepoint on Day 1, and the result remained positive at subsequent assessments in Study Part 1.

## ORION-16 HeFH

Clinical laboratory evaluations, physical findings, and other observations

During Part 1, there were no clinically meaningful changes or between-group differences in safety laboratory parameters of haematology or biochemistry (including liver enzymes, CK, FPG, HbA1c). Also, no adverse effects regarding growth (height, weight, BMI), pubertal development (Tanner staging), or hormonal status (oestradiol in females, testosterone in males, LH, FSH, DHEA-S, cortisol) were seen with inclisiran treatment. There were no clinically meaningful differences in sex hormones between inclisiran and placebo groups over the course of Part 1 and the results for sex hormones and Tanner stage in males and females were consistent with the expected progression through puberty in the age groups studied. Height and body weight increased similarly over time in the inclisiran and placebo treatment groups; mean (±SD) height increases from baseline to Day 330 in the inclisiran and placebo groups were 2.7 cm (±2.88) and 2.7 cm (±2.90), respectively, and these changes occurred from similar mean baseline heights of 163.0 cm and 165.4 cm. Consistent increases were also seen for body weight, while mean changes in BMI were minimal in both treatment groups.

In Part 2, there were also no clinically meaningful changes in safety laboratory parameters of haematology or biochemistry, and no adverse effects regarding growth, pubertal development, or hormonal status were seen with inclisiran treatment. There were no notable changes from baseline in sex hormones over the course of Part 2 and the results for sex hormones and Tanner stage in males and females were consistent with the expected progression. Regarding Tanner assessment at EoS (Day 720), in males, for genitalia stage, the percentage of participants who either had a change in Tanner stage or were already at the maximum Tanner stage at baseline was 90.2% in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 91.7% in the placebo-inclisiran group; for pubic hair stage, this was 85.4% and 91.7%, respectively. In females, for breast development stage, the percentage of participants who either had a change in Tanner stage or were already at the maximum Tanner stage at baseline was 94.0% in the inclisiran-inclisiran and 95.8% in the placebo-inclisiran group; for pubic hair stage, this was 96.0% and 91.7%, respectively. Height and body weight increased over time during the 2-year study duration. The mean (±SD) height increase at EoS (Day 720) was 4.5 cm (±4.92) in the inclisiran-inclisiran (mean baseline 163.0 cm) and 5.3 cm (±5.99) in the placebo-inclisiran group (mean baseline 165.4 cm). Consistent increases were also seen for body weight, while mean changes in BMI were small.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Immunogenicity (ADA measurements)

For the analysis of formation of ADA to inclisiran, whole blood study samples samples were collected in part 1 on Day 1 prior to study drug (inclisiran or placebo) injection (referred to as Day 1 0h) and 4 hours after injection (referred to as Day 1 +4h), as well as on Day 150 and Day 330. Unscheduled samples and Early Termination (ET) samples collected prior to Day 360 (due to unscheduled visits or early termination/premature discontinuation) were also collected and analysed.

In Part 2 (Year 2), samples for the ADA analysis collected on Day 510 and Day 720.

Whole blood study samples from the ORION-16 Clinical Study No. CKJX839C12301 were processed into serum and recovered serum study samples were shipped to the Test Site on dry ice for analysis.

## Part 1 (Double-blind period)

The frequency of participants experiencing treatment-induced persistent and transient ADA is presented in the table below. Persistent and transient ADA were assessed using the definitions proposed by Shankar et al (2014). There were no persistent or transient treatment-induced ADA responses reported in either the inclisiran or the placebo treatment group. In addition, no participant had a positive ADA result at any of the measured timepoints (Day 1 pre-dose, Day 1 4h post-dose, Day 150 and Day 330).

## Part 2 (Open-label period)

The frequency of participants experiencing treatment-induced persistent and transient ADA is presented in the table below. As for Study Part 1 (double-blind period), ADA was assessed using the definitions proposed by Shankar et al (2014). In the vast majority of participants (94.2%, 131/139), no treatment-induced ADA responses were reported, (i.e., the participants remained ADA negative). Out of the 139 participants analysed, 8 participants (5.8%) showed a positive ADA response, 5/91 (5.5%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran group and 3/48 (6.3%) in the placebo-inclisiran group. All positive samples were of low titre.

| Category                            | Inclisiran-Inclisiran (N=91) n/m (%) (*)   | Placebo-Inclisiran (N=48) n/m (%) (*)   | Total (N=139) n/m (%) (*)   |

Transient: Treatment-induced ADA detected at only 1 time point post-baseline, or 2 or more sampling time points where the first and last ADA-positive samples are separated by a period less than 16 weeks, and the subject's last sampling time point is ADA-negative.

(Source: ORION-16 CSR Table 14.3-7.1ii]

The incidence of treatment-induced transient ADA was 1.4% (2/139; 0% in the inclisiran-inclisiran group and 4.2% (2/48) in the placebo-inclisiran group), and the incidence of treatment-induced persistent ADA was 4.3% (6/139; 5.5% (5/91) in the inclisiran-inclisiran group and 2.1% (1/48) in the placebo-inclisiran group). Two of the 6 responses classified as persistent were observed at a single timepoint (EoS), while for the remaining 4 cases, positive responses were seen at 2 timepoints.

The difference in ADA detected between Study Part 2 (very low incidence of treatment-induced ADA) and Study Part 1 (no treatment-induced ADA) could be attributed to analytical variability of the different cross-validated method used in Study Part 2 (see section on methods above for details).

In study ORION-16, no participants developed ADAs during study Part 1. During Part 2, 8/139 participants (5.8%) showed a positive ADA response (5.5% inclisiran-inclisiran, 6.3% placeboinclisiran). The difference in ADA detected between study Part 2 (low incidence of treatment-induced ADA) and Part 1 (no treatment-induced ADA) could be attributed to analytical variability of the different cross-validated method used in Part 2 (see section 8.2.1 Methods - Immunogenicity above).

Importantly, for ADA positive participants in study Part 2, although detection of ADA may have been slightly more sensitive, titres were low. No clinically meaningful differences in the efficacy and safety of inclisiran were observed in participants who tested ADA positive or negative.

## Supportive safety data

ORION-5 HoFH

In both parts, there were no cases of clinically significant haematology parameters.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In Part 1, there were no cases of clinically significant serum creatinine (≥50% increase from baseline and/or &gt;2 mg/dL) or CK levels (&gt;5x ULN) in either group. There were also no cases of potentially clinically significant changes in HbA1c (≥6.5% and ≥0.5% change from baseline). In Part 2, there were no cases of serum creatinine &gt;2 mg/dL; elevated serum creatinine ≥50% increase from baseline was observed in 2 participants (3.8%; inclisiran-inclisiran group). There were no cases of clinically significant CK levels (&gt;5xULN). The incidence of potentially clinically significant changes in HbA1c (≥6.5% and ≥0.5% change from baseline) was 7.5% (5.9% inclisiran-inclisiran, 10.5% placeboinclisiran).

In Part 1, no participant in the inclisiran and one (5.3%; 1/19) in the placebo group had a clinically significant biomarker for hepatic function, increases in ALT and AST &gt;3x and ≤5x ULN; there were no notable elevations in ALP or total bilirubin and also no cases meeting the definition of Hy's law in either group. In Part 2, 2 participants (3.8%) showed clinically significant biomarkers for hepatic function (1 inclisiran-inclisiran increase in ALT &gt;3x and ≤5x ULN, 1 placebo-inclisiran increases in ALT and AST &gt;3x and ≤5x ULN). There were no notable elevations in ALP and total bilirubin and no cases meeting

the definition of Hy's law. In Part 1, there were no urinary tract infections. In Part 2, 1 participant (inclisiran-inclisiran) experienced an SAE of urinary tract infection, which was not considered related to study drug and fully recovered. Anti-inclisiran antibodies were not detected in any of the samples [Study A12302 Section 11.6]. Vital signs (SBP, DBP, heart rate) were comparable between the two treatment groups. Since study ORION-5 was conducted in adult patients, assessments of growth, Tanner stage, and

menarche status were not evaluated.

## Safety in special populations

Due to the small sample size, no subgroup analyses based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors were performed in study ORION-13. In addition, no specific extrinsic factors were considered important for inclisiran.

In study ORION-16, subgroup analyses by gender, age group (≥12 to &lt;15 and ≥15 to &lt;18 years of age), race, and BMI were performed for all treatment-emergent AEs, AEs leading to study drug discontinuation, AEs related to study drug and separately for injection site reactions. There were generally no clinically relevant differences in the AE profile between subgroups. In the inclisiran group, injection site reactions AEs were reported more frequently in in the younger (24.4%; 11/45) compared to the older age group (8.3%; 4/48), but in the placebo group, injection site reaction AEs were reported more frequently in the older (12.0%; 3/25) compared to the younger age group (0%; 0/23). Additionally, in the older age group, injection site reaction AEs (known ADR) were reported more frequently in the placebo (12.0%; 3/25) compared to the inclisiran group (8.3%; 4/48). In Part 2, no clinically relevant differences were seen in the AE profile between subgroups. Interpretation of these data is limited by the small sample size and the 2:1 randomisation ratio in Part 1. No specific extrinsic factors were considered important for inclisiran.

## Safety related to drug-drug interactions and other interactions

No new drug-drug interaction studies with inclisiran have been performed in support of this application.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Discontinuation due to adverse events

## ORION-13 HoFH

No AEs leading to study drug discontinuation were reported in either Part of the study.

## ORION-16 HeFH

During Part 1, AEs that led to study drug discontinuation were reported for 1 participant (1.1%) in the inclisiran group; in addition to a transient (1.5 hours) mild injection site reaction, this participant experienced AEs of mild vertigo and nausea, starting on the day of the second study drug injection (Day 90), which were reported to have led to study drug discontinuation. These symptoms were accompanied by mild fatigue and headache and, starting two days later, moderate abdominal pain. The participant fully recovered 4 days after onset of the first AE. No AEs that led to study drug discontinuation were reported for Part 2.

## Supportive safety data

In both studies, ORION-5 and VICTORION-PEDS-OLE, no AEs that led to study drug discontinuation were reported.

## Post marketing experience

Inclisiran is currently approved for use in adults and the post-marketing data is limited to the adult population.

The most recent PSUR for inclisiran covered the period from 01 January 2024 to 31 December 2024. Up to this cut-off date, worldwide cumulative patient exposure since the IBD of 09 December 2020 is estimated to be approximately 368,582 patient treatment years. For the PSUR reporting interval, the exposure to inclisiran from post-marketing experience was 233,286 patient treatment years. The assessment of post-marketing usage for inclisiran remained consistent with the known safety profile.

## 2.5.1. Discussion on clinical safety

## HoFH

Inclisiran is currently only licensed in adults, including those with HeFH, but not with HoFH, since in the phase III, two-part (double-blind placebo-controlled / open-label), multicentre study ORION-5 (MDCOPCS-17-02) in adult patients with HoFH, inclisiran treatment failed to reduce LDL-C levels despite substantial lowering of PCSK9 levels 10 .

## Assessment of paediatric data on clinical safety

No new or unexpected safety signals have been reported from study ORION-13 in adolescents from ≥12 to &lt;18 years of age with HoFH; the sparse safety data (13 participants overall, 9 inclisiran, 4 placebo) in this population gathered from both parts of the study are considered to be in line with the known general safety profile in adult patients, but it has to be kept in mind that inclisiran has not been licensed in adults with HoFH (see above). No adverse effects regarding growth, pubertal development, or hormonal status were seen with inclisiran treatment in either period of the study.

10 Raal F, Durst R, Bi R, Talloczy Z, Maheux P, Lesogor A, Kastelein JJP; ORION-5 Study Investigators. Efficacy, Safety, and Tolerability of Inclisiran in Patients With Homozygous Familial Hypercholesterolemia: Results From the ORION-5 Randomised Clinical Trial. Circulation. 2024 Jan 30;149(5):354-362. doi: 10.1161/CIRCULATIONAHA.122.063460. Epub 2023 Oct 18. PMID: 37850379; PMCID: PMC10815002

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

However, interpretation of the provided data is limited by the very low number of participants included in study ORION-13 (see above) as well as by the open-label uncontrolled design of Part 2. No definite conclusions on the safety of inclisiran in adolescents with HoFH can be drawn from these sparse data. As regards the supportive safety information from study ORION-5 in adult patients with HoFH, the study also included only 56 adult participants, placebo-controlled data are limited to 6 months and Leqvio is not authorised in adults with HoFH.

## HeFH

As regards adolescents from ≥12 to &lt;18 years of age with HeFH, study ORION-16 (CKJX839C12301) included 141 participants (93 inclisiran, 48 placebo). The safety data in this population gathered from both parts of the study are considered to be in line with the known safety profile of inclisiran in adult patients and no new or unexpected safety signals have been observed. There were 5 events of syncope in the inclisiran group, which were considered not related to study drug. The applicant has provided clarification on the possible reasons for 5 events of syncope in the inclisiran group and it was agreed that there is currently no indication of a causal relationship to inclisiran treatment. No adverse effects regarding growth, pubertal development, or hormonal status were seen with inclisiran treatment in either period of the study. Differences in the number of ADA between Part 1 (no treatment-induced ADA) and Part 2 (positive ADA response in 5/91 (5.5%) in the inclisiran-inclisiran group and 3/48 (6.3%) in the placebo-inclisiran group) of Study ORION-16 were further discussed. The Applicant clarified that the observed difference in the number of ADA between Part 1 and Part 2 could be related to analytical variability of the different cross-validated method used in Study Part 2. The changes and modification of the ADA assay resulted in a more sensitive assay potentially resulting in a slight increase in positive ADA readout, reducing the probability of false negative readout. Importantly, for participants ADA-positive in study Part 2 / Year 2, although the detection of ADA may have been slightly more sensitive, titers were low. To note, there were no clinically meaningful differences in the efficacy and safety of inclisiran in participants who tested ADA positive or negative. The Applicant discussed, based on the available data from adults, if there is a risk of developing ADA increases with the duration of inclisiran treatment. The applicant argued that the available data do not indicate that the risk of developing anti-drug antibodies (ADA) to inclisiran increases with the duration of inclisiran treatment and that there is no substantiative immune response with GalNAc-conjugated siRNA's such as inclisiran. This was agreed by CHMP.

Overall, interpretation of the provided safety data was limited by the low number of participants (n=141) included in study ORION-16.

In addition, supportive safety data from the ongoing extension study VICTORION-PEDS-OLE available so far, do not indicate new or unexpected safety signals.

## Additional expert consultations

N/A

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.5.2. Conclusions on clinical safety

In conclusion, safety data from study ORION-13 in adolescents from ≥12 to &lt;18 years of age with HoFH are limited.

The provided safety data from study ORION-16 in adolescents from ≥12 to &lt;18 years of age with HeFH do not indicate any new or unexpected safety issues and, although very limited, together with the established safety profile of inclisiran in the adult population are considered adequate for the risk evaluation in this patient population. The overall safety of inclisiran in in paediatric patients aged 12 years and older with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HeFH) can be considered satisfactorily addressed.

## 2.5.3. PSUR cycle

The requirements for submission of periodic safety update reports for this medicinal product are set out in the list of Union reference dates (EURD list) provided for under Article 107c(7) of Directive 2001/83/EC and any subsequent updates published on the European medicines web-portal.

The results of this user consultation demonstrated that the PL meets the criteria for readability as set out in the EU Guideline on the readability of the labelling and package leaflet of medicinal products for human use (v1, 12 January 2009).
