## 3. Benefit-Risk Balance

## 3.1. Therapeutic Context

## 3.1.1. Disease or condition

Familial hypercholesterolaemia (FH) is a genetic disorder of lipoprotein metabolism manifested by lifelong and marked elevation in LDL-C levels from birth. If left untreated, it is characterised by premature cardiovascular disease (CVD). FH is caused by variants in genes encoding proteins which regulate LDL Receptor (LDLR)-mediated clearance of LDL-Cholesterol (LDL-C); there are two clinical manifestations, Heterozygous FH (HeFH) and the more severe Homozygous (HoFH), depending on the presence of one or two affected alleles in these genes.

## 3.1.2. Available therapies and unmet medical need

The underlying atherosclerotic vascular lesions that cause CVD, and peripheral vascular occlusive disease are caused by an elevated LDL-C level. An outstanding role to reduce the risk of major CV events is lowering of LDL-C, which has been established for a long time. Data to support the relationship between LDL-C and cardiovascular (CV) events and specifically the relationship with inhibition of Proprotein Convertase Subtilisin/Kexin Type 9 (PCSK9) has been shown in genetic studies of variations in the genes that influence LDL-C and from studies in patients with FH. In Mendelian randomisation studies evaluating multiple genetic targets that impact LDL-C levels, the variants in genes that regulate LDL-C levels (e.g., LDL-R, PCSK9) have demonstrated that lowering of LDL-C is associated with a lower risk for CV events. To minimise this risk factor, lipid-lowering therapies are recommended. To reach this goal, specific targets have been suggested. It has been shown that the higher the CV risks, the more aggressive the lipid-lowering therapy should be.

Early initiation of treatment in patients with FH is considered crucial for Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease (ASCVD) risk reduction (Reijman et al 2021). Despite the availability of multiple options of lipid-lowering therapies, including statins, ezetimibe, and newer treatments like anti-PCSK9 or antiANGPTL3 (HoFH only) monoclonal antibodies, many patients with HeFH or HoFH, including adolescents, do not reach recommended LDL-C goals and a relevant unmet medical need is considered to remain, in particular for the young patient population, who is at high risk of future CV events and thus requires intensive management of hypercholesterolaemia. In addition, as adherence to lipid-lowering therapies is known to be a particular challenge in adolescents (Joyce et al 2016, Langslet et al 2021, Weinstock et al 2023), effective treatments to reduce LDL-C, which do not require frequent self-administration, might be especially beneficial in this age group.

## 3.1.3. Main clinical studies

Inclisiran was first approved in the EU on 09 December 2020 under the proprietary name of Leqvio and is currently only licensed in adults, including patients with heterozygous, but not with homozygous FH, from 18 years of age onwards.

## Paediatric data

The completed paediatric studies ORION-16 (CKJX839C12301) and ORION-13 (CKJX839C12302) are part of agreed Paediatric Investigation Plan (PIP) for inclisiran and were intended to investigate the use of inclisiran in adolescents from ≥12 to &lt;18 years of age with HoFH (ORION-13) or HeFH (ORION-16) and elevated LDL-cholesterol.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Both were conducted as two-part, multicentre, randomised studies; Part 1 (year 1) investigated inclisiran versus placebo in a double-blind setting, while in Part 2 (year 2) all participants received open-label inclisiran. Inclisiran was applied at the licensed standard dose and posology for adults.

## HeFH / ORION-16

The study population consisted of adolescents ≥12 to &lt;18 years with a genetic or clinical diagnosis of HeFH, an LDL-C &gt;130 mg/dL (3.4 mmol/L), and being on a maximally tolerated dose of statin at the investigator's discretion with or without other lipid lowering therapy (excluding PCSK9 inhibitors). Patients with HoFH or secondary hypercholesterolaemia were excluded.

The initial planned sample size was approximately 150 adolescents which was significantly reduced to at least 102 participants in a protocol amendment in October 2022. A sample size of ≥102 participants allowed only for detecting a ≥30% reduction in LDL-C in the chosen setting.

During the double-blind Part 1 (year 1) of the study, the primary efficacy and safety data comparing inclisiran to placebo were collected. The open-label single arm Part 2 (year 2) of the study allowed the collection of longer-term data on inclisiran, with a focus on longer-term safety. Study ORION-16 also included PK and pharmacogenetic assessments; PK assessment was based on sparse PK sampling (pre-dose, 4-, 24-hours after first dose).

The primary endpoint was the percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to the end of the doubleblind treatment period at day 330 (year 1). Secondary endpoints included absolute change in LDL-C and other lipid parameters during Part 1 as well as assessments from baseline to the end of the openlabel Part 2, i.e. day 720. Exploratory endpoints included individual responsiveness of participants, changes in LDL-C by type of underlying causal mutation, change in carotid intima-media thickness (cIMT), and evaluation of inclisiran pharmacokinetics (PK) based on sparse data. The endpoints are considered to be in line with previous adult inclisiran studies.

## HoFH / ORION-13

No formal hypothesis testing was planned and none was performed for study ORION-13. There was also no formal sample size calculation; the sample size was selected based on feasibility.

The study included 13 male and female adolescents, 9 in the inclisiran and 4 in the placebo group, with a genetic confirmation of HoFH, excluding LDLR null/null genotypes, and an LDL-C &gt;130 mg/dL who were on a maximally tolerated dose of a statin, with or without other lipid lowering therapy (except PCSK9 inhibitors, mipomersen, lomitapide). Patients with HeFH or secondary hypercholesterolemia were excluded. One (1) participant in the placebo group was on LDL-apheresis. All participants completed both parts of the study and were analysed.

During the double-blind Part 1 (year 1) of the study, the primary efficacy and safety data comparing inclisiran to placebo were collected. The open-label single arm Part 2 (year 2) of the study collected longer-term data on inclisiran with a focus on longer-term safety. To explore PK of inclisiran in adolescents with HoFH, the study also included PK and pharmacogenetic assessments.

The primary endpoint was the percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to the end of the doubleblind treatment period at day 330 (year 1). Secondary endpoints included time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330, absolute changes in LDL-C and other lipid parameters during Part 1, as well as assessments from baseline to the end of the open-label Part

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

2, i.e. day 720. Exploratory endpoints included individual responsiveness of participants, changes in LDL-C by type of underlying causal mutation, change in cIMT, reduced frequency of LDL-apheresis treatment at Day 720 (Year 2) compared to baseline, and evaluation of inclisiran pharmacokinetics (PK) based on sparse data. The endpoints are considered to be in line with previous inclisiran studies in adults.

## 3.2. Favourable effects

## ORION-16 HeFH

In the primary analysis, study ORION-16 met the primary objective of demonstrating superiority of inclisiran compared to placebo in reducing LDL-C in adolescents with HeFH on maximally tolerated statin treatment. The effect of a between-group difference (95% CI) of -28.54% (-35.81, -21.27; p&lt;0.0001) between inclisiran and placebo is considered clinically relevant.

Results of sensitivity and subgroup analyses were consistent with the primary analysis.

As regards key secondary efficacy endpoints, in Part 1 of the study, relevant reductions were seen for time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 330; the betweengroup difference in mean percent change was -29.30% (-36.24, -22.36; p&lt;0.0001).

Relevant reductions were also seen for absolute change in LDL-C, and percent change in Apo B, nonHDL-C, and total cholesterol (all p-values &lt;0.0001), while results for Lp(a) were not statistically significant (p-value 0.1419) and , given the hierarchical testing of the key secondary endpoints, further results are to be considered exploratory.

Results of pre-specified sensitivity and subgroup analyses for the key secondary endpoints were consistent with the primary analysis.

During the open-label Part 2, consistent reductions were seen in LDL-C, sustained over 2 years of treatment. The mean percent change in LDL-C (±SD) from baseline to Day 720 was -33.7% (±24.0).

As regards exploratory results, 56.7% (51/90) in the inclisiran versus 12.5% (6/48) in placebo group reached an LDL-C &lt;130 mg/dL and 27.8% (25/90) versus none, respectively, an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. A ≥50% reduction in LDL-C was reached by 16/90 (17.8%) in the inclisiran and none in the placebo group.

During the open-label Part 2, 90/139 (64.7%) participants reached an LDL-C &lt;130 mg/dL and 53/139 (38.1%) an LDL-C &lt;100 mg/dL. A ≥20% reduction in LDL-C was reached by 105/139 (75.5%), ≥30% by 85/139 (61.2%), and ≥50% by 33/139 (23.7%) participants.

Consistent LDL-C lowering was seen with inclisiran across the included genetic variant groups in Part 1. For the open-label Part 2, such an analysis was not specified in the protocol but added for completeness in the SAP; as for Part 1, consistent LDL-C lowering was seen across the included genetic variant groups.

No clinically relevant effect of inclisiran on cIMT was seen during Part 1 or 2.

## ORION-13 HoFH

For study ORION-13 no formal hypothesis testing was planned. As regards primary analysis, the mean percent change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 was -21.6% in the inclisiran and +11.7% in the placebo group (mean between-group difference [95% CI] -33.25% [-59.17, -7.34]). A supplementary analysis excluding the participant (n=1) on LDL-apheresis showed consistent results.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A waterfall plot of individual percentage changes in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330 by participant showed a variable magnitude; all participants in the inclisiran group showed a reduction in LDL-C albeit with significant differences in magnitude, while response in the placebo group was less consistent with an increase in LDL-C of 54.9%, as well as a decrease of -15.4%.

Two thirds (66.7%) of the participants in the inclisiran group achieved a &gt;15% and more than half (55.6%) a ≥20% reduction in LDL-C from baseline to Day 330.

In the secondary analyses, in line with the primary endpoint, the mean time-adjusted percent change in LDL-C (after Day 90 up to Day 330) was -21.0% in the inclisiran and 13.0% in the placebo group (between-group difference [95% CI] -33.97% [-67.54, -0.39]). A supplementary analysis excluding the participant on apheresis showed consistent results.

The percentage changes from baseline in LDL-C in Part 1 were largely consistent across visits, with isolated outliers; the participant in the placebo group treated with LDL-apheresis showed some visit-tovisit fluctuation. Reductions were seen for absolute change in LDL-C and percent change in ApoB, nonHDL-C, VLDL-C, and total cholesterol.

No reduction was seen in Lp(a) and only small variable effects for triglycerides, HDL-C, and Apo A1.

As regards Part 2, there was a lower extent of the overall mean change in LDL-C compared to Part 1; the difference between treatments groups was driven by 2 participants in the inclisiran-inclisiran group who had increases in LDL-C of +27.3% and +47.4%, respectively. A waterfall plot of percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 720 showed a variable magnitude; 10/13 participants had a decrease in LDL-C at Day 720, while in 3/13 participants, LDL-C increased at Day 720.

## 3.2.1. Uncertainties and limitations about favourable effects

## ORION-16 HeFH

As regards the use of inclisiran in adolescents with HeFH, study ORION-16 included only 141 participants (93 inclisiran, 48 placebo) and placebo-controlled data are limited to 1 year of treatment.

## ORION-13 HoFH

As regards the use of inclisiran in adolescents with HoFH, the Applicant has provided only data from a very small study in 13 paediatric patients (9 inclisiran, 4 placebo) with an exploratory design. The provided results indicate heterogenous effects, including LDL-C increases in patients treated with inclisiran, in this patient population and no criteria are available to distinguish between patients who might or might not profit from treatment with inclisiran. Due to very limited number of HoFH subjects included it is not possible to conclude on efficacy in this population of patients.

Furthermore, inclisiran is not licensed in adults with HoFH since study ORION-5 (MDCO-PCS-17-02) failed to show an effect in this patient population (Raal et al., 2024).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 3.3. Unfavourable effects

The safety of inclisiran in adults has been characterised and assessed from the Phase III development program, including data from 1833 adults with ASCVD, ASCVD risk equivalents, or HeFH exposed to inclisiran on top of maximally tolerated statin therapy. Data from these studies and from relevant extension studies indicate a favourable safety profile in adults. A higher incidence of injection site reactions is an identified common adverse drug reactions (ADR) of inclisiran (8.2% inclisiran, 1.8% placebo); these were mild or moderate in severity, transient, and resolved without sequelae. There were more frequent elevations of serum hepatic transaminases &gt;1x and ≤3x ULN in patients on inclisiran (ALT: 19.7%, AST: 17.2%) than on placebo (ALT: 13.6%, AST: 11.1%); these elevations did not progress to &gt;3x ULN, were asymptomatic, and not associated with adverse reactions or other evidence of liver dysfunction. Confirmed Anti-Drug Antibodies (ADA) positivity was detected in 1.8% prior to dosing and 4.9% during 18 months of treatment with inclisiran; no clinically significant differences in the clinical efficacy, safety, or pharmacodynamic profiles of inclisiran were observed in the patients who tested positive for anti-inclisiran ADAs. No overall differences in safety were observed between these elderly and younger patients. Inclisiran is not associated with an increased risk for renal dysfunction, hypersensitivity, or neurologic events / neurocognitive disorders, and there was no difference from placebo in new onset or worsening of diabetes.

For the current application, the existing safety data in adults have been complemented by safety data from the use of inclisiran in the treatment of adolescents with HoFH or HeFH (ORION-16 or ORION-13, respectively). Furthermore, supportive safety data have been provided from study ORION-5 in adults with HoFH, an indication that has not been licensed, and from the ongoing extension study VICTORION-PEDS-OLE in adolescents.

As regards adolescents with HeFH, study ORION-16, the safety data gathered in this population are considered to be in line with the known safety profile of inclisiran in adult patients and no new or unexpected safety signals have been observed. No adverse effects regarding growth, pubertal development, or hormonal status were seen with inclisiran treatment in either period of the study.

No new or unexpected safety signals have been reported from study ORION-13 in adolescents with HoFH; the sparse safety data (13 participants overall, 9 inclisiran, 4 placebo) in this population gathered from both parts of the study are considered to be in line with the known general safety profile in adult patients. No adverse effects regarding growth, pubertal development, or hormonal status were seen with inclisiran treatment in either period of the study.

In addition, supportive safety data in adolescents with either HoFH or HeFH from the ongoing extension study VICTORION-PEDS-OLE available so far, do not indicate new or unexpected safety signals.

## 3.4. Uncertainties and limitations about unfavourable effects

Interpretation of the provided safety data from study ORION-16 in adolescents with HeFH, these are also based on a limited sample size of 141 participants (93 inclisiran, 48 placebo). While inclisiran safety profile in adolescents with HeFH can be considered overall addressed, the observed difference in the number of ADA between Part 1 and Part 2 in ORION-16 study were initially of concern. could be related to analytical variability of the different cross-validated method used in Study Part 2. The changes and modification of the ADA assay resulted in a more sensitive assay potentially resulting in a slight increase in positive ADA readout, reducing the probability of false negative readout. Importantly, for participants ADA-positive in study Part 2 / Year 2, although the detection of ADA may have been slightly more sensitive, titers were low. To note, there were no clinically meaningful differences in the efficacy and safety of inclisiran in participants who tested ADA positive or negative.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Interpretation of the provided safety data in adolescents with HoFH (ORION-13) is limited by the very low number of participants (N=13; 9 inclisiran, 4 placebo) included in the study. Furthermore, inclisiran has not been licensed in adults with HoFH and the supportive safety information from the failed study ORION-5 in adults with HoFH also encompass only 56 adult participants and the placebocontrolled data from this study are limited to 6 months of treatment.

The provided supportive safety data from the ongoing open-label, single arm, multicentre extension study VICTORION-PEDS-OLE in participants with HoFH or HeFH who have completed studies ORION-13 or ORION-16, are limited by the age of participants included. Since this is an extension study, a considerable part of the population is already in the adult age range (mean age at time of entry 17.1 years; 40.8% ≥18 years of age). Furthermore, the trial includes only participants who derived a benefit in the investigator's judgement.

## 3.5. Benefit-risk assessment and discussion

## 3.5.1. Importance of favourable and unfavourable effects

Significant and consistent reduction of LDL-C is considered to be of outstanding importance for the prevention of CHD, CVD, and peripheral vascular occlusive disease and lipid-lowering therapies are recommended in patients with HeFH or HoFH. Furthermore, early initiation of treatment in patients with these conditions is considered crucial for ASCVD risk reduction. Despite the availability of multiple options of lipid-lowering therapies, many patients with HeFH or HoFH, including adolescents, do not reach recommended LDL-C goals and a relevant unmet medical need is considered to remain.

Inclisiran shows a higher incidence of injection site reactions compared to placebo; however, after initiation of treatment, injections are limited to twice a year and the events were mild or moderate in severity, transient, and resolved without sequelae. Elevations of serum hepatic transaminases could be indicators of potential liver disease; however, elevations seen with inclisiran treatment did not progress to &gt;3x ULN, were asymptomatic, and not associated with adverse reactions or other evidence of liver dysfunction. Development of ADAs could potentially influence efficacy or safety of treatment, but so far, no clinically significant differences in the clinical efficacy, safety, or pharmacodynamic profiles of inclisiran were observed in the patients who tested positive for anti-inclisiran ADAs. Furthermore, inclisiran is not associated with an increased risk for renal dysfunction, hypersensitivity, or neurologic events / neurocognitive disorders, and there was no difference from placebo in new onset or worsening of diabetes. The safety data provided within this application do not indicate new or unexpected safety issues in adolescents with HoFH or HeFH. No adverse effects regarding growth, pubertal development, or hormonal status were seen with inclisiran treatment in either period of the study. The sparse safety data gathered from both studies are considered to be in line with the known general safety profile in adult patients.

## 3.5.2. Balance of benefits and risks

As regards the use of inclisiran in paediatric patients aged 12 years and older with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HeFH), the efficacy data of the pivotal Study ORION-16 together with the available efficacy data from the use of inclisiran in the adult population with HeFH, are, in general, considered to support the proposed relevant change to the indication:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

in paediatric patients aged 12 years and older with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HeFH): in combination with a statin or statin with other lipid-lowering therapies in patients unable to reach LDL-C goals with the maximum tolerated dose of a statin, or alone or in combination with other lipid-lowering therapies in patients who are statin-intolerant, or for whom a statin is contraindicated.

The safety data provided do not indicate any new or unexpected safety isssues and, although very limited, together with the established safety profile of inclisiran in the adult population are considered to support the preliminary risk evaluation in this patient population. In summary, the benefit-risk balance for the use of inclisiran in adolescents with HeFH was considered positive.

As regards the use of inclisiran in adolescents with HoFH (study ORION-13), the available efficacy data are considered insufficient to support the proposed indication. The very limited sample size, exploratory design, heterogeneity of response, and lack of supportive efficacy findings from the adult HoFH study ORION-5 result in substantial remaining uncertainty regarding efficacy in this population. During the procedure, the MAH confirmed that they no longer wish to pursue the proposed indication in paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HoFH). Nevertheless, a concise summary of study results was included in sections 4.8, 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC.

## 3.5.3. Additional considerations on the benefit-risk balance

N/A

## 3.6. Conclusions

The overall B/R of Leqvio is positive.

## 4. Recommendations

## Outcome

Based on the review of the submitted data, this application regarding the following change:

| Variation(s) requested   | Variation(s) requested                                                                                                                                 | Type              |
|--------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|
| C.I.6.a                  | Addition of a new therapeutic indication or modification of an approved one                                                                            | Variation type II |
| C.4                      | Change(s) in the summary of product characteristics, labelling or package leaflet due to new quality, preclinical, clinical or pharmacovigilance data. | Variation type II |

Grouping of two Type II variations to support the extension of the indication to paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia, and to include update to sections 4.8, 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC concerning treatment of paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia:

C.I.6: Extension of indication to include the treatment of paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

years with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HeFH) for based on the final results from study CKJX839C12301 (ORION-16). ORION-16 is a two part (double-blind inclisiran versus placebo [Year 1] followed by open-label inclisiran [Year 2]) randomised multicentre study to evaluate safety, tolerability, and efficacy of inclisiran in paediatric patients (12 to less than 18 years) with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia and elevated LDL-cholesterol. As a consequence of this variation, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.8, 5.1, and 5.2 of the SmPC are updated. The Package Leaflet is updated in accordance.

C.4: Update to sections 4.8, 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC to include the treatment of paediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia (HoFH) based on the final results from study CKJX839C12302 (ORION-13). ORION-13 is a two part (double-blind inclisiran versus placebo [Year 1] followed by open-label inclisiran [Year 2]) randomised multicentre study to evaluate safety, tolerability, and efficacy of inclisiran in paediatric patients (12 to less than 18 years) with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia and elevated LDL-cholesterol. The Package Leaflet is updated in accordance.

Version 4.1 of the RMP has also been submitted.

The group of variations leads to amendments to the annex(es) I, and IIIB and to the Risk Management Plan (RMP).

## Amendments to the marketing authorisation

In view of the data submitted with the group of variations, amendments to Annexes I and IIIB and to the Risk Management Plan are recommended.

## Conditions or restrictions with regard to the safe and effective use of the medicinal product

## · Risk management plan (RMP)

The MAH shall perform the required pharmacovigilance activities and interventions detailed in the agreed RMP presented in Module 1.8.2 of the Marketing Authorisation and any agreed subsequent updates of the RMP.

In addition, an updated RMP should be submitted:

At the request of the European Medicines Agency;

Whenever the risk management system is modified, especially as the result of new information being received that may lead to a significant change to the benefit/risk profile or as the result of an important (pharmacovigilance or risk minimisation) milestone being reached.

## Paediatric data

The requirements for the submitted dossier in relation to paediatrics are described in section 1 of this report.

In this procedure, the CHMP reviewed the available paediatric data from studies conducted in accordance with the agreed Paediatric Investigation Plan P/0321/2018 (and its subsequent modifications, on 2 December 2022 (EMA decision P/0495/2022) and on 11 August 2023 (EMA decision P/0322/2023)). The results of these studies are reflected in the Summary of Product Characteristics (SmPC) and, as appropriate, the Package Leaflet.
